Its ÍNDICE DE LAS LÁMINAS (djvu.txt line ~21887): lámina IV = "Plano de las excavaciones hechas en el sitio de Numancia en 1853" (the plate's own OCR reads 1853/55 - confirm on the image) + "Trozo de muro descubierto en el sitio de Numancia" + "Portada de la ermita de los Mártires de Garray" + Calatañazor rock tombs + Augustóbriga walls; láminas I/II/V = the road map, the bridges/calzada sections/inscriptions, pottery. The plates sit between the work's ERRATAS and the next memoir (1439 truces) in the djvu stream.   zero appends, so test 3 saw x2 missing and the canonical files unchanged. Fix:
  `f.write("".join(new_lines).encode("utf-8"))` (the lines came from utf-8 text reads, so the
  re-encoding is byte-identical).

  the `fit/` directory -> FileNotFoundError (tests 1-2). Added the mkdir.

  mangle wiltshire_b/van_deventer (PITFALLS entry filed); (b) `hashes()` hashed paths relative

  lines against `splitlines()` (no `\n`) so canon_before was ALWAYS empty (and startswith on
  an empty prefix passed trivially) - fixed with rstrip("\n"); (d) growth-count comprehension
  lacked the exists() guard for the absent roman staging. Also deleted dead code left in the

  canonical conflicts (so the distinct-row growth count is exact for this data).

# Task 213 artist-views

## 2026-10-11 213 DECISION: staged rows use hand-recorded caption_coords evidence; record --live is the wrong route (traps confirmed live)
`fit/evidence.py:source_tables()` only reads `pipeline/sources.py:SOURCES`, and fit/evidence.py is not in my
Touches, so a `hand_tied` row for "fit/src_artists.py" would FAIL check. I hand-record the `caption_coords`
field in caption_route's key order (rec/kind/checked/named_place/place_qid/place_label/place_lon/place_lat/
site_lon/site_lat/dist_m). `record` skips rows that already carry rec==evidence/1, so 211's later record runs
leave them alone. Prep OQ-1 (a byte-identical live record for Venice) is unachievable: `record --live` resolves
the caption by NAME SEARCH, and --verify confirmed the first hit with P625 is the trap for BOTH sites: search
"Imola" -> Q50195 itself, which carries the bad P625 (11.7147, 44.3531), 53 km NW -> conflict -> kind none;

## 2026-10-11 213 DECISION: the prep's Imola "centre 44.245 N, 12.367 E / bad P625 53 km NW" was wrong - that point is Cervia; P625 is the correct city point
The prep notes placed Imola's old town at 44.245 N, 12.367 E and, seeing Q50195's P625 (44.353, 11.715) far
from it, called the P625 "bad, ~53 km NW of the town". Verified live (2026-10-11): the prep point is CERVIA
on the Ravenna coast (~50 km SE of Imola); the real city is at 44.353 N, 11.715 E, where THREE independent
sources agree: the enwiki infobox (44°21'11"N 11°42'53"E), Wikidata P625, and the OSM town node (with the F1
circuit beside it and the Santerno - "Fiume Santerno" at 44.3606/11.7104 - running through the urban area).
So the "53 km trap" was the distance between two wrong values. Consequences: (a) the site point is the
walled town's centre, 11.7112/44.3535 (Piazza del Duomo, OSM) - the centre of the sheet's circular view, not
the modern city point; (b) the prep's "Savena crossing" was a second error - the drawing's river is the
Santerno, which Leonardo labels "Sant'Arno" (mirror script, read off the sheet); the view is south-up per
the literature; (c) the evidence story changes: for Imola the live record route resolves the RIGHT QID (the
hand record still stands to pin the walled centre, deterministic and offline); the name-search trap that is
REAL and documented is Venice (first hit with P625 = Q773853 Venice, California, 9891 km). Side note: OSM's
comune boundary for Imola is mis-drawn (Nominatim displays the urban area as "Imola, Nuovo Circondario
Imolese", and the admin relation's centre sits 8 km north of the city) - a reason never to geocode Imola
from OSM/Nominatim. The PITFALLS entry "Q50195 (Imola) carries a bad P625..." must be corrected accordingly
(pending in the next session; I cannot edit PITFALLS from the context-limit hand-over step).

Session 1 on 217: investigation only, no code (full plan is in the task's Hand-over). Decisions made, each with
its evidence:
1. **The module mirrors 213's hand list, the file stays the dump.** `HAND_FIGS["civitatesorbiste00brau"]` will
   hold the 121-line pipe table (fig|title|page|kind|kind_by) transcribed verbatim from
   data/books/lists/civitatesorbiste00brau.json (pre-generated at /tmp/hand_figs_table.txt); `parse_hand_figures`
   + `figure_list` (OCR-parsed or hand) feed cmd_ocrmap/validate_book/list_pages. `fig` = the leaf's own n, so
   hand books need no page-number mapping (pagenum_map/map_plates untouched).

   ensure_ascii=False` (raw Æ - verified: dumps(parsed, indent=0, ensure_ascii=False) == file, and NOT equal
   with the default); four of the other eight books' files contain `\uXXXX` escapes (default writer). So
   cmd_ocrmap dumps `ensure_ascii=(ia_id not in HAND_FIGS)` for both .json and .map.json; all other bytes
   (indent=0, key order fig,title,page,kind,kind_by) reproduce 213's file and 216's eight byte-identical files.
3. **The `view` field is the row contract for 211 and the page.** cmd_index adds `"view"` to rows of
   HAND_FIGS books only (true = kind-o town view, false = kind-s plan, true = model-flagged leaf not in the
   list); placed right after "page_type" via a `**{}` spread so plan-book rows get NO new key and stay
   byte-identical (box 2's "shape unaffected"). found_by gains the tag "list_view" (mirrors "list"). The page's
   plans.json already has a `view` column (bool, set from Clef for Commons bird's-eye pictures) - the index
   field is the book-row feed for it; 211's merge/export maps it (their lane, noted in the hand-over).

   view:true + 7 view:false; 890 plan-book rows byte-identical; baseline run saved: /tmp/idx_committed,

   (listed 121; recall_pages 0.286 = 2/7 of the hand plans {90,237} of the 11 model-flagged; stage_A_only 3).

   stage") I read as a warning against subset runs, and the 211 hand-over already treats "zero civitates rows

   works for all 10 foldout leaves (44, 86, 116, 168, 230, 238, 254, 346, 360, 412); ia.get returned real

   (foldout 86 = the Burgdorff view, confirmed by image); (b) they are still absent from ia.pages(), so
   they have no n, no triage image, no index row - plates living ONLY on a foldout leaf are transcribed
   with fig='' and the index simply never sees them (no leaf to point at; no fabricating a row on a
   neighboring leaf). Foldout 44 turned out to be a duplicate TEXT page (p19, 'Beschreibung der
   Endgnoschafft'), not a plate; several portrait-sized foldouts (168, 230, 238, 346, 360) may be text
   duplicates too; 412 is 3744x5616 (possibly a double-page spread). Check each image before use.

   list_c12-14/c32/c33/list_lower). 75 lines / 81 plates; names + printed page numbers as in the task
   hand-over. Corrections vs session 3's draft: 'Genff' is GENEVA (not Griffensee) with its own im
   Grund/im Prospect pair; 'Neuenburg' carries a handwritten '2 Ansichten' (2 plates); 'Pfeffers' and

# Task 219 source-line-year

## 2026-10-12 219 DECISION: 302c no longer blocks - the lead dropped 302c/302d in main d39ead8
The task's `Depends on: 302c` existed only because both touched pipeline/plan_fit.py. The lead's
commit d39ead8 (main, 2026-10-12) dropped 302c/302d as superseded by the matcher fit and set 219's
Depends line to `none`. 302c never made its design changes to plan_fit.py (its own hand-over:
investigation only, no code), so nothing from it collides with 219's edit; plan_fit.py is
byte-identical between my branch and main (merge-base e2ca3d9; main's only commit since is d39ead8,
task files + journals only). Proceeded on this branch without waiting.

## 2026-10-12 219 DECISION: year-once rule = drop the label parenthesis whenever the title carries any 4-digit year
The task's two sentences ("drop the label's parenthesis when the title already contains that year"
vs "only a title without a year shows the label (or 'undated')") diverge for the 3 docs whose
label-year differs from the title-year (argentoratum 1831/1836, bethlehem 1881/1882, mound-bottom
1923/1924). Implemented the stricter, unambiguous one: the parenthesis appears ONLY for a yearless
title (label or "undated"); a titled year always drops it. The label is not lost from the page: it
stays in the entry's `scale` field. The year check reuses the same 4-digit pattern as the entry's
sort year (_year4) so the two can never disagree; non-4-digit numbers ("116", "0154", 5-digit ids,
"17th century") are not years, so "(1750-1900)"-style labels still show under yearless titles.

## 2026-10-12 219 DECISION: refactor source_line extraction is byte-identical for all 133 store docs
Extracted the citation + who/how clause from build() into pure source_line(doc, n, m, who) and the
year regex into _year4 (committed 4b93f5c). Proof: a throwaway script evaluated the old inline
expressions (copied verbatim from HEAD) and the new function for every doc in data/db/platefits
(133) with m from similarity(doc['pairs']) unscaled - 0 mismatches. The least-squares similarity is
scale-equivariant (scaling all u,v by the picture's fs scales a back by 1/fs, leaving the ground
residuals the same), so m and corners do not depend on which image size stands in for the picture;
this is why the hermetic test can build with a stand-in image of the doc's own frame size (fs=1)
and still pin the exact how-clause.

## 2026-10-12 219 DECISION: re-bake = plan_fit.build per stale entry, then restore sites.json byte-for-byte
The one-off re-bake (14 built year-titled commons depictions + shotwick, which had never been
built in this checkout) ran the product's own path: plan_fit.build(doc) per doc, root = this
checkout. Contrary to the previous hand-over's expectation, every big picture was already
cached in data/raw/commons/big/ -> zero downloads. Proof that "only the string changes": for
all 14 the rebuilt entry equals the stored one in every field except source; a second
identical run is byte-stable (the fixed state is a fixed point); shotwick's rebuilt entry
matches agent a's built entry (corners, fit, year, label, scale, coverage, kind, file, ink)
with only the source fixed. Each build's trailing build_site.set_drawings refreshed
web/data/sites.json (tracked, in 306's Touches) - the committed bookkeeping was stale (5
sites: drawings 0 despite built plans, bethlehem 0->12) - and I restored the committed
bytes: a gitignored build does not change committed bookkeeping (agent a's checkout follows
the same convention); the product refreshes the file on its own next time. The naive
verification check ("(label)" in source) false-positives on 3 of the 15 (avaricum, bolsover,
castle-rising: the label string occurs inside the title itself) - the structural test is
source.split(", Wikimedia Commons:")[0] == title (PITFALLS).

# Task 300 site-stonehenge

## 2026-10-11 300 DECISION: the one-site line (tools/site_done.py) reuses batch_us.run_sites only for UNCOVERED candidates, and needs three root-aware refactors (plan_fit, build_site, scoped review.export)
Where: tools/site_done.py (new), pipeline/plan_fit.py, pipeline/build_site.py, fit/review.py
Rationale: measured state - Stonehenge's 13 plans.json rows all have a verdict (7 auto docs, 6 in its
review item, sets disjoint), so running run_sites blindly (even model=None) would not fit anything new
but its write_item upsert would REPLACE the 6 rich batch-written candidate entries (gate scores, model
pairs, why) with "no model answer" stubs (upsert merges by whole-entry replacement, PITFALLS fit/).
So site_done's fit step runs run_sites only when a candidate has neither doc nor item entry (or the
site has no candidates at all, no pre-fit and no item - the no-picture case). The existing steps were
not root-aware: plan_fit and build_site derive every path from their own file location, and
review.export records the write-once resolved field into EVERY derivably-resolved item (today exactly
one other site: alligator-effigy-mound-q4732645) - a one-site command must not touch other sites'
files, hence export(root, site_id=None) (scoped record, same page file) and _root(root=None) threading
through plan_fit (build/main/prune/load_picture + new is_done) and build_site (drawings/set_drawings/
write_entry). serve.py calls plan_fit.main([]) - default args must stay behaviour-identical.
Also fixed in plan_fit while in there: the `how` string claims "Placed by hand" for automatic fits
(wrong on the public page; auto docs get "Fitted automatically by the batch ... (gate score X m)",
hand-fit strings unchanged), and load_picture's bare urllib has no Wikimedia pacing/Retry-After
(7 back-to-back downloads 429, PITFALLS ~L602) - new _fetch helper paces ~1 rps, honours Retry-After,
backoffs on 5xx. Baseline before all changes: 59 passed / 0 failed (59.76 s). Not yet implemented at
hand-over: the design is fully specced in the task's Hand-over; next session writes the code, then the
real run (7 Commons downloads expected), then a re-run for idempotency, then tests/browser/docs.

## 2026-10-11 300 DECISION: the one-site line is implemented and has taken Stonehenge to the map; one session remains (tests/test_site_done.py)
Where: tools/site_done.py (new), pipeline/plan_fit.py, pipeline/build_site.py, fit/review.py, docs/site_flow.md, docs/site_flow/stonehenge.png, web/data/{sites.json,review.json,sites/stonehenge-q39671/}, data/raw/commons/big/
Rationale: iteration 65 implemented exactly the previous session's design and ran the line on the real
`tree with no model flag. Results, all verified on disk: (1) fit - "13 candidate picture(s): 7 fitted (doc),
6 in the review item -> no batch run needed" (the covered/uncovered distinction worked: run_sites was never
called, so no item entry was clobbered); (2) sync - 7 documents synced, each big picture downloaded from
Commons through the new paced _fetch (14 requests, no 429, ~35 s); (3) entry - drawings 0 -> 7; (4) review -
the item published (339 open of 340 - the batch's population, unchanged), no `resolved` recorded anywhere
(stonehenge's 6 candidates have no resolving doc; the scoped export also left alligator's derived-resolved
item untouched - all 340 item files byte-identical, all 130 platefits docs byte-identical, only the stonehenge
entry in sites.json changed, hand-fit golden green, suite 59 green both before and after). Re-run of the
command: every doc "skipped: overlay already current", sites.json and platefits byte-identical - idempotent.
Browser check (box 3): serve.py --port 0 + shot.mjs with `select('stonehenge-q39671')` - probe reports
drawings 7, marker class without `plain` (the square is coloured), the 1800 Rijksmuseum plan auto-selected
and rendered in red ink exactly on the real sarsen circles over the aerial photograph; screenshot committed
as docs/site_flow/stonehenge.png with its one-line description in docs/site_flow.md.
What broke this session (all fixed, all documented in docs/site_flow.md for the next site): the entry line
first printed "drawings 7 -> 7" because plan_fit.build calls set_drawings after every fit, so a "before" read
taken AFTER the sync sees the final value - the before is now captured before the sync. (The 429 risk did

exit 0, the per-picture lines, depictions.json byte-equal to the real repo's, entry 0 -> 7, and every other

## 2026-10-11 300 DECISION: tests/test_site_done.py runs the copied one-site line on a copy of Stonehenge's files with a socket-dead subprocess and big pictures at the REAL cache dimensions; task done
Where: tests/test_site_done.py (new)
Rationale: box 4, the last box. The test builds a throwaway tree in tmp_path: the code copied whole
(tools/, fit/, pipeline/ - site_done resolves its data root from its own path, so the copied code runs on

<!-- task 301 -->
# Task 301 site-argentoratum

## 2026-10-11 301 DECISION: lead redirect - Stonehenge is not done first; its 7 batch fits are all fabricated frame-to-box identity maps, and the gate passed them by construction
The lead (mid-session, 2026-10-11): before Argentoratum, check each of Stonehenge's 7 synced drawings; anything visibly off goes to the review queue with the reason; find why the gate passed the Rijksmuseum plan (16.7 m, 2-3x too big, off-centre) and make the LINE catch it, with a test; correct the false sentence in docs/site_flow.md; then Argentoratum.
Root cause (verified this session): all 7 stonehenge auto docs (c_0226 23.2 m, c_098c 7.4 m, c_20f9 44.1 m, c_6933 26.8 m, c_83b4 16.7 m, c_897a 38.0 m, c_f838 0.0 m) are the model's documented fabrication class (batch_us.llm_model docstring, pilots 4a/4c: "internally perfect regular patterns (identity maps, corner-to-corner, diagonal ladders)"): regular ladders of round page-px coordinates (u 200-1080 step ~200-400, v 150-1200 step ~100-200) mapped to regular ground ladders (points on shared lon/lat lines 100-450 m from the site centre; 4 of the 7 have one anchor within ~1 m of the site's NOMINAL centre - the catalog coordinate, not a feature), implied scale 0.80-1.07 m/px = box width / page px (the model treated the page frame as the 1000x1000 m site box). Why the gate passed it: run_sites never asks the model for a placement - it SOLVES the similarity from the model's own pairs and the gate scores that least-squares fit against the same points, so the residuals are only the ladder's ~20-25% anisotropy + jitter (med 0-44 m < 45, slope_o 0.02-0.12 < 0.145, no radial pattern) -> ACCEPT. That is gate.md section 7's registered limit verbatim ("a consistent global error passes"; the gate does not re-verify the anchors are the right features) - the gate did its registered job. The batch's three pre-gate counterchecks all pass BY CONSTRUCTION on this class: the stated-scale cross-check compares the model's stated scale with its own ladder's implied scale (they agree by construction), and _feature_present passes because ladder points scattered over structureless Salisbury Plain sit at/above the 400-point null median (the next-door candidate c_ffb3d drew the 25% verdict). All 7 verified visually (composites over relief.jpg, /tmp/stonehenge_overlay_check.png): none sits on the sarsens - c_83b4 is 2-3x too big AND ~70 m off-centre (the lead's exact description), the other six likewise off in scale and/or position. Consequence the lead accepts: all 13 of Stonehenge's candidates end in the review item (human's drag is the last word) and the site shows 0 drawings until a human fits at least one.

## 2026-10-11 301 DECISION: the line's fix - a fabrication-pattern check (2D lattice / 1D ladder / frame-identity) before sync; pattern docs go to the review item with the reason, never to the map
Chosen over extending the frozen gate (that would void the gate.md registration and test_gate pins its constants) and over re-running the batch (the batch's pre-gate checks are exactly what passed by construction; the line is the last gate before the map and re-validates each site's docs on its turn - no batch-wide re-run needed). Shape (detailed in the task hand-over): a new pure function in pipeline/plan_fit.py, `fabrication_pattern(pairs, crop, size, box) -> None | reason`, three conditions any one of which fires: (a) 2D lattice (>=3 distinct u AND >=3 distinct v, consecutive gaps near-arithmetic on both axes, >=4 anchors) - fires on c_098c, c_83b4; (b) 1D ladder (>=3 anchors collinear with near-arithmetic spacing) - fires on c_0226; (c) frame-identity (the solved similarity maps the crop frame onto the site box: centre within 25% of the box side per axis, size ratio per axis in [0.7, 1.4]) - fires on c_20f9, c_6933, c_897a, c_f838. site_done._sync runs it before any download; on a hit: no build, prune any existing overlay (plan_fit.prune), upsert a review-item candidate carrying the doc's pairs + gate score + the pattern reason, recompute the entry and republish the queue. Tolerances must be validated to fire on all 7 stonehenge docs and on NONE of the 20 hand-fit docs (the golden reference). Test: extend tests/test_site_done.py in its existing hermetic harness (socket-dead child, tmp copy). Then run the line on stonehenge (no model needed: all 13 candidates covered -> no batch run, no network: pattern docs are rejected before any fetch), fix site_flow.md + replace stonehenge.png, then argentoratum.

### 2026-10-11 301 DECISION: the line's fabrication check = standalone grid-roundness plus 2D-lattice and 1D-ladder clauses on the doc's page-px pairs; frame-identity dropped, so the function takes pairs only
Chosen of the two options the previous hand-over left open: standalone grid-roundness as the primary clause (its own recommendation: a strict gate that over-refuses is fine, the human's drag is the last word, and it fires on ALL 8 argentoratum docs, not just the 5 the frame-identity+round variant caught). Frame-identity is dropped entirely: it fires on 4 legitimate hand maps (avebury/bergues/calleva/camp-d "the drawing covers the site box"; rotation cannot separate them, tested last session) and adds no coverage (roundness already fires all 15 targets). With frame-identity gone, crop/size/box are dead params, so the implemented signature is fabrication_pattern(pairs) in pipeline/plan_fit.py (pure numpy, no download; runs on the doc's stored pairs in the picture's size frame, before any fetch). (b) is kept because it is not redundant: pontefract c_3213 (22% round) is a 1D ladder - the union of the three clauses fires on EVERY batch auto doc in the repo (110/110 in data/db/platefits), not just the 15 targets.
Validation (the real function, run this session): fires 15/15 stonehenge + argentoratum auto docs - c_098c fires all three (9/10 per-axis-distinct round, lattice gaps 1.091/0.909 u + 1.125/0.893 v, two 3-anchor ladders) - and fires 110/110 repo batch docs; fires 0/20 hand fits (tests/golden/hand_fit_docs.sha256). Nearest hand margins: (a) whitley max/median 1.3066 vs 1.3, (b) castle-rising min/median 0.801 vs 0.9, (c) maiden 0.43 vs 0.9. The previous hand-over's c_098c "9/10" was per-axis-distinct (u distinct + v distinct); union-distinct would be 7/8 = 0.875 and would NOT fire (c) on c_098c - the implementation uses per-axis, the convention that lets (c) alone fire all 15. The 10-px roundness: the fit sheet's grid labels sit at 100/200/500/1000 source px (fit/batch_us._step/_grid), so a model reading labels lands on multiples of 10 px; the page tool records a human's drag with one decimal. The check is independent of the batch's three pre-gate checks (re-read llm_model/_parse_answer/_feature_present: range drop, stated-scale, texture - none look at page-px coordinate patterns).

## 2026-10-11 302a DECISION: frozen configuration of fit/inkfit.py (recorded BEFORE the first fit run; 10-10 pre-registration discipline - bug fixes allowed later, any parameter change must be labelled exploratory and reported separately)
Session 3: investigation only (all modules the method imports were read in full; llm.py confirmed to have no import-time network; skimage 0.26.0 import-verified in .venv). The method's parameters are frozen here, on a priori ground (reused from the documented 10-10 run), before any site is fitted; the smoke run (next session) happens AFTER this entry. No parameter below may be chosen or changed using any of the 19 held-out sites.
- Extractor: pipeline/autowalls.py P unchanged (docs/autofit.md section 3): sigmas (4,6,9), ridge_thr 0.25, gamma 0.013, coh_sigma 25.0, coh_min 0.6, min_len 50, min_extent 50, normal_sigma 4.0, step 2, margin 20 px. Applied to the picture's panel crop (map_box, or full frame) in the picture's own pixel frame; points later rescaled to the doc's size frame by im.width/size[0].
- No-model fallback rule: NO blanking boxes (the 10-10 auto_noqwen behaviour; the 50-px length filter already drops most lettering) and KEEP ALL surviving pieces. A model (duck-typed: .boxes(im) -> 0-1000-normalised non-map boxes, .label_pieces(im, pieces) -> per-piece kind) may blank non-map boxes and keep only wall-labelled pieces; an absent/unusable answer falls back to the no-model rule and is labelled as such in the result.
- Seed: fit/propose.propose_for (scale bar > ratio at recorded ppi > picture-size; north arrow unless |deg|>60 or unrecorded -> north-up; centre = site centre, i.e. t0 = (0,0) in relief-box-centre metres). Fallback when propose raises (no scale at all): max(size_m)/max(panel px extent), north-up, site centre.
- Search window (reused unchanged from pipeline/autofit.py's frozen 10-10 constants): rotation +-25 deg, scale x0.74..x1.36, centre +-200 m; coarse FFT on an absolute grid ROT_STEP 2 deg, LOGS_STEP 0.04, K_BINS 8 direction bins, CELL_M 4.0 m, NEG_FLOOR -0.2 m; refine = pattern search (0.02/1 deg/4 m -> 0.01/0.5/2 -> 0.005/0.25/1), n_refine 8 candidates, single seed via fit_site with the seed dict at scale_factor 1 / offset 0.
- Objective (selection): mean over template points of max(contrast, -0.2 m); contrast = fit_plate's ridge contrast (relief on the line minus the HIGHER of the two sides side_m away); LRM = aw.local_relief(radius_m 24), clipped +-1.0 m, modern_mask and water_mask set to NaN (fit_plate's defaults; the per-site hand knobs in data/sites/<id>/site.json are hand-fit artefacts and are NEVER read by the method); >= 50% of points on valid relief else score -9.
- Relief: box = aw.site_bbox(site.lon, site.lat, site.size_m or 1000x1000 m), px_m = 1.0 (all held-out and smoke sites have 1 m-class sources, verified in session 1); loader order data/inkfit/relief/<site>.npz (z, px_m, bbox; shape-checked against the expected w x h) -> data/sites/<site>/dem.npy -> aw.fetch_dem (working phase only).
- Null (search-matched, docs/autofit.md finding 2): null = max of the SAME search (identical seed/window/grid/n_refine) over (a) the scrambled template - each piece rigidly rotated U(0,2pi) and translated by U(-0.4,0.4) of the panel extent, clamped inside, all from np.random.default_rng(20261012) - reusing the same Coarse (unchanged ground); and (b) the DEM shifted by half the box north and half east (ndi.shift order=0, cval=NaN; new Coarse). Fewer than 100 valid draws in a null search is recorded (newark's 10-10 problem); a -9 objective counts low.
- Confidence: obj_fit - null (both on the floored-mean objective, metres). The sync-vs-review threshold is NOT set here: box 3 authorises setting it on 302b's held-out table (302c applies it).
- Emitted pairs (anti-fabrication by construction, plan_fit.fabrication_pattern must be silent): <= 2 sampled points per kept piece at FRACTIONAL positions 0.23 and 0.71 (never a fixed stride: clause (b) fires on >=3 anchors sharing one coordinate within 2 px with near-arithmetic spacing on the other), longest pieces first, max 10, min 4 (fewer -> "not enough wall ink" to review); mapped to lon/lat through the best placement; rounded 1 dp px / 7 dp lon-lat (the doc convention). If fabrication_pattern ever fires, re-draw with default_rng(20261012 + attempt) up to 8 times, else the pattern string is the review reason. fit_gate.gate runs on the pairs as a defence layer (it accepts by construction: residuals 0 - gate section 7's documented consistent-global-error limit).
- Output document: batch_us.write_doc shape (site/plan/crop/pairs/label/title/cite/ia/file/book or src:commons url/ref/size; auto true; score = the gate's median residual) plus "fitter": "ink". pipeline/plan_fit.build reads all of those without breaking today; its "how" string is still the batch wording (keyed on auto:true alone) - the wording fix is 302c's (plan_fit.py is in the parent's Touches, not this task's).

## 2026-10-11 302a DECISION: placement->similarity geometry verified; b convention is complex(u0c, -v0c) (MINUS-v) - the session-3 script's +v0c was an 860 m near-miss
Session 4: re-ran the session-3 verification script (kept in the iter-0075 log) and found it had a SECOND bug beyond the noted broadcast fix: it applied M to [du, dv] instead of the autofit convention d = [du, -(v - v0)]. With both fixed (d = [u-u0c, -(v-v0c)], en = d @ M.T + t; lon/lat = aw.to_lonlat(cx + e/k, cy + n/k), k = 1/aw.ground_scale(lat)):
- plan_fit.similarity recovers a = k*s*exp(i*rot) to relative error 7e-6 (= the 1 dp px / 7 dp lon-lat rounding limit of the synthetic pairs), residuals 1.4-4.6 mm; fit_gate.gate accepts (med 3 mm).
- **b = (cx + k*tE) + i(cy + k*tN) - a*complex(u0c, -v0c)** — the minus-v form. The session-3 script used complex(u0c, +v0c) and reported a relative error of 1.3e-4, which looked fine only because |b| ~ 6.6e6 m absorbs the 860 m disagreement. Cross-checked both ways: the solved b matches zc - a*complex(u0,-v0) to the rounding limit, and re-fitting propose._anchor_pairs' 5 pairs recovers that anchor's a and b exactly (a = (m_per_px/ground_scale)*e^(i*rot), b = zc - a*complex(u0, -v0)).
fit/inkfit.py's placement_of and map_pairs must use the minus-v w0 and the flipped-v d; the rest of the design stands as recorded in the task file.

## 2026-10-11 302a DECISION: session 6 - the method verified end to end (hermetic + 2 real sites); two bug fixes; boxes 1 and 3 ticked
Session 6 (after the driver committed the session-5 working tree as a1291a6): thinned-thickened the synthetic plate, found and fixed two real bugs, completed the hermetic run, and smoke-ran both real sites in the working phase. All parameters still exactly the frozen registration; both fixes below are bug fixes, not tuning.
- BUG FIX (module): template_pieces returned `_keep_pieces(...)` as its first element, but that function returns a (list, label) TUPLE - so fit() received pieces = (list_of_dicts, "") and the iteration blew up with "list indices must be integers or slices, not str" the moment a non-empty piece list was produced. It was masked in session 5 because every run so far had early-returned at "no wall ink extracted" (empty list = falsy). Fixed by unpacking inside template_pieces (kept, label = _keep_pieces(...); return kept, _variant(model) + label, notes).
- BUG FIX (CLI hygiene): the CLI printed the search progress and aw's fetch chatter (overpass retries) to stdout, corrupting the JSON result file. main() now redirects sys.stdout to sys.stderr around the fit() call (print() resolves sys.stdout dynamically, so this catches aw.py's own prints too) and emits only the JSON document on stdout. No changes outside fit/inkfit.py.
- Hermetic run (the /tmp/aw-inkfit-herm/root fixture, recreated this session): the 8 px sharp drawing was NOT the fix - a razor-sharp 0/1 step makes the frozen Frangi (gamma 0.013, calibrated on soft real-scan edges) ghost-echo a ring ~5-8 px OUTSIDE the drawn walls (even inside the zeroed margin), and a blurred 8 px line falls under the ridge threshold. The scan-realistic fixture (8 px rectangle + 12 px interior line, gaussian_filter 2.5 px, +-2/255 paper noise, rng seed 7; 12 px is inside the real 6-20 px wall band) gives exactly the 3 true centre lines, no ghosts. fit() then ran fully offline in 8.1 s: 3 pieces / 81 template points, seed 0.75 (the hermetic picture-size fallback - propose_for has no catalog in the temp root, as designed), search landed on the TRUE placement (s 0.7408, rot -0.5 deg, t [-17, 0] vs true 0.75/0/0), both nulls valid, gate accept (n 6, 4 mm), fabrication silent, doc returned with fitter:"ink" and batch_us's field shape. CONFIDENCE WAS NEGATIVE (-0.025): fit 0.7152 vs scramble 0.7404; the shifted-DEM null was -9 (invalid: shifting the 120x100 m box half its size leaves a 60x40 m valid region smaller than the 120x90 m template - recorded by design, shifted_valid:false). Two toy-geometry lessons for 302c's committed test site: (a) make the site box much larger than the earthwork (a 1000 m-class box with a ~100 m earthwork) so the half-box shift keeps >= 50% of the template valid; (b) a template of one or two long straight pieces keeps scramble signal (a long straight wall re-aligned to any one ridge side still scores) - a fixture with several pieces of different lengths/angles makes the null discriminating. The method ANSWERED "not confident" on the toy site - that is the decision layer working, not a failure.
- Smoke run 1 (real, working phase): stonehenge-q39671 / earthworkenglan01allcgoog_n0582_1 (NOT among the 19 held-out sites - checked against tests/golden/hand_fit_sites.txt). 134.5 s: 19 pieces / 1952 template points from the real plate; seed 0.5034 m/px (picture_size; the 64.3 deg north hint rejected as |deg|>60, north-up) as predicted in session 5; search found a FALSE peak at the window edge (s 0.3716 = x0.738 of seed, rot +25.5 deg = the window bound, t [177, -149] m) exactly as docs/autofit.md finding 1 predicted; nulls: scramble 0.1227 > fit 0.0468, shifted 0.0114 -> null 0.1227, confidence -0.0759; the method therefore reports "not confident" (302c sends it to review, never to the map); gate accept (n 10, 12 mm); fabrication silent (10 irregular pairs); corners inside the 1000x1000 m site box. OSM modern mask: 3 overpass 504 retries, then success (no "modern mask unavailable" note); DEM fetched to data/sites/stonehenge-q39671/dem.npy (8.0 MB, gitignored).
- Smoke run 2 (real, working phase): pyramid-mound-q7263260 / ancientmonuments00squi_n0134_1 (also not held-out). 74.4 s: 4 pieces / 273 points; seed 0.8806 (picture_size, north-up); placement s 0.752, rot 10 deg, t [-123, -197] (inside the window, near the seed); fit 0.311 vs scramble 0.442 (null) vs shifted 0.1703 -> confidence -0.131, again "not confident"; gate accept (n 8, 4 mm); fabrication silent; 8 pairs; corners inside the 800x800 m box. DEM 5.1 MB to data/sites/pyramid-mound-q7263260/dem.npy.
- Both smoke sites answered "not confident" - CONSISTENT with the 10-10 pre-registration (the relief score has false peaks; only a search-matched null can say so) and with this task's remit: box 1 asks for the machinery end to end with a placement + pairs, which both runs deliver, and neither site is a held-out site, so nothing here tuned a parameter. The held-out table (302b) will show whether the margin is positive on sites where the hand fit is truth; if the no-model variant stays negative there, that is the reported result (302 says "report the numbers as they are").
- Suite: .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -> 59 passed / 1 failed (the pre-existing tests/test_gate.py::test_recorded_measurement_reproduces_and_agrees, owned by 218 - gate_heldout expects 20 hand-fit docs but the lead's 21st doc is on main). No new failures from this module.
- BOX STATUS: 1 (two real non-held-out sites, end to end, no platefit read) TICKED; 2 (scikit-image pinned) done in session 2; 3 (hermetically testable: injectable model + seeded npz relief loader) TICKED - the loader validated shape/px_m/bbox and the whole method ran offline; the committed test-site npz is 302c's build; 4 (frozen configuration recorded) done in session 3; 5 (tests/ untouched) HOLDS - this task wrote no test files.
- PITFALLS: new entry under pipeline/autowalls.py (the frozen ink extractor) - Frangi ghost-echoes razor-sharp synthetic drawings; soften fixtures to scan-like edges (blur 2.5, 8-12 px walls, light seeded paper noise).

The section registers: the 21 sorted no-auto doc ids (the 20 golden + the lead's 21st, all verified auto=None in this checkout after the driver's main sync), the frozen config of fit/inkfit.py @ f7dbba6 (extractor per docs/autofit.md section 3, no-model variant - the AI box is not reachable from this VM, relief 1 m/px with 24 m LRM clipped to +/-1 m, propose anchor seed, window +-25 deg / x0.74..x1.36 / +-200 m, 2 deg / 0.04-log / 4 m coarse grid with 8-candidate refine, mean(max(contrast,-0.2)) objective with -9.0 floor, the search-matched null = max of the scrambled-template (seed 20261012) and DEM half-box-shifted searches, the 0.23/0.71-quantile point recipe with 8 fabrication-pattern redraws, the frozen gate, one-shot checkpointing into data/inkfit/heldout/results.json), the corner-error metric (both similarities in the doc's size-frame Mercator convention, RMS over the 4 full-image corners in ground metres via aw.ground_scale(lat0)), rotation error and scale ratio, and the statement that the method never reads the hand pairs (the evaluator fit/inkfit_heldout.py is the only reader). Caught before committing: my first draft converted Mercator->ground with 1/aw.ground_scale(lat0); verified numerically that aw.ground_scale(lat) IS "ground metres per Mercator metre" (= cos lat, 0.6335 at 50.69 deg), so the protocol correctly uses aw.ground_scale(lat0) directly (inkfit.map_pairs divides because it goes ground->Mercator the other way). Table summary (21 rows, 19 sites, no-model variant, run 2026-10-11T22:47:37Z, method
f7dbba6):
- 20/21 docs produced a fit; the frozen gate accepted all 20 (its documented limit - a
  consistent global error passes, docs/gate.md section 2). camp-d-attila (commons)
  produced none: not enough wall ink.
- Corner error of the 20 fits: min 231.2 m (berkhamsted), median 540.0 m, mean 755.0 m,
  max 2203.6 m (calleva). Rotation error -126.1..+97.9 deg; scale ratio 0.2378..13.214.
  Against site size (err/size): 13 fits within half the site's size (badbury 0.165,
  cadbury 0.236, hembury 0.265, berkhamsted 0.289, maiden-castle-commons 0.335, cahokia
  0.346, avebury 0.376, castle-rising 0.391, serpent-mound 0.399, old-sarum-book 0.448,
  aztalan 0.453, mayburgh 0.469, little-solsbury 0.490); 7 beyond it (mound-bottom
  0.611, bergues 0.641, whitley 0.674, old-sarum-commons 0.712, maiden-castle-book
  0.725, calleva 1.377, arbor-low 1.399 - the last two place the plate about a whole
  site-size away). No two-doc site (old-sarum, maiden-castle) is good on both docs.
- Which sites fit and which did not: best berkhamsted (231.2 m, 29% of size) and badbury
  (264.5 m, 17% - also the best margin); worst calleva (1.38x size) and arbor-low
  (1.40x). "Fit" at <= 0.5x size: 13 of 20 docs.
- What it means for the 302c threshold: the null margin does NOT separate good fits from
  bad ones. Sorted by margin, badbury +0.1871 (best, 264.5 m) and calleva +0.1070 (worst,
  2203.6 m) are both comfortably positive, and calleva's margin is ABOVE castle-rising's
  +0.0806 (313.2 m) and old-sarum-book's +0.0693 (717.3 m). A threshold > 0 passes 11
  docs (worst 2203.6 m); > 0.05 passes 6 (maiden-castle-book 1160.5 m and calleva among
  them); > 0.10 passes 2 (badbury and the worst, calleva); only a threshold in
  (0.1070, 0.1871] passes exactly one doc - badbury, the best. So the threshold 302c
  sets on this table can only ration the pass rate; it is not an accuracy guarantee at
  any value. The honest expectation for the 302d Stonehenge run: the ink fit almost
  always goes to the review queue with its reason, and places nothing on the map unless
  the margin clears the threshold - that outcome gets reported plainly, not papered over.

Boxes 2-5 ticked, Status: done. Next: 302c (decision + tools/site_done.py wiring + e2e
test); the 302b hand-over lists what is frozen and must not be touched.

  bad ones. Sorted by margin, badbury +0.1871 (best, 264.5 m) and calleva +0.1070 (worst,
  2203.6 m) are both comfortably positive, and calleva's margin is ABOVE castle-rising's
  +0.0806 (313.2 m) and old-sarum-book's +0.0693 (717.3 m). A threshold > 0 passes 11
  docs (worst 2203.6 m); > 0.05 passes 6 (maiden-castle-book 1160.5 m and calleva among
  them); > 0.10 passes 2 (badbury and the worst, calleva); only a threshold in
  (0.1070, 0.1871] passes exactly one doc - badbury, the best. So the threshold 302c
  sets on this table can only ration the pass rate; it is not an accuracy guarantee at
  any value. The honest expectation for the 302d Stonehenge run: the ink fit almost
  always goes to the review queue with its reason, and places nothing on the map unless
  the margin clears the threshold - that outcome gets reported plainly, not papered over.

Boxes 2-5 ticked, Status: done. Next: 302c (decision + tools/site_done.py wiring + e2e
test); the 302b hand-over lists what is frozen and must not be touched.


<!-- task 302c -->
# Task 302c decision-wiring

## 2026-10-12 302c DECISION: the confidence threshold is 0.15, set on the 302b held-out table (box 1) - the null margin only rations the pass rate; it is not an accuracy guarantee
First session on 302c (iteration 84): investigation and design only, no code written (the context
limit hit before the first edit). The value and the design, frozen for the next session:
- Threshold CONF_THRESHOLD = 0.15 on the search-matched-null margin (confidence = objective_fit
  - max(scramble, shifted) as fit/inkfit.py already computes). Rationale from the 302b table
  (docs/site_flow.md "Measured one-shot"): sorted margins run -0.5150..+0.1871 and do NOT
  separate good from bad fits (worst calleva +0.1070/2203.6 m sits above castle-rising +0.0806
  and old-sarum-book +0.0693). A threshold > 0 passes 11 docs, > 0.05 passes 6, > 0.10 passes 2
  (badbury AND the worst, calleva); only a threshold in (0.1070, 0.1871] passes exactly one
  document - badbury, the best (264.5 m, 17% of the site's size). 0.15 is mid-interval: 0.043 of
  room above calleva's rounded 0.1070 and 0.037 below badbury's 0.1871, robust to small DEM/OSM
  drift between the held-out run and a re-run. A future change to the value is a method change:
  it must be labelled and re-measured, never a silent fix.
- Decision (applied in fit/inkfit.py, additive): confident iff the fit produced reportable pairs
  AND the frozen gate accepted them (verdict == fit_gate.ACCEPT) AND fabrication_pattern is
  silent AND the null ran AND margin >= 0.15. Everything else is unsure with a
  human-readable reason (the existing no-fit reasons are already human-readable; new: a gate
  refusal names the median residual; a below-threshold margin names the margin, the fit and
  null objectives and which null won - per the 302a hand-over note). Confident -> automatic
  platefit document (auto: true, fitter: "ink", the gate's score); unsure -> the site's review
  item with the reason and the best starting pairs, never to the map.
- New result fields are "confident" (bool) and "unsure_reason" (str|None). The "reason" field
  keeps its exact meaning (None on the success path): inkfit_heldout.measure() stores r["reason"]
  into the frozen one-shot checkpoint (data/inkfit/heldout/results.json), so routing the margin
  message through "reason" would corrupt the checkpoint if measure were ever re-run. "confident"/
  "unsure_reason" are new keys measure() does not store, so the checkpoint and the verbatim table
  stay untouched (verify: `fit/inkfit_heldout.py table` byte-identical to the committed section).
- Task file: Touches extended to docs/site_flow.md (the decision paragraph goes next to the
table), data/inkfit/ (the committed test-site relief npz seeds under data/inkfit/relief/, the
committed real-plate copies under data/inkfit/plates/) and tests/test_site_done.py (the
no-model CLI message of site_done changes wording, and that test asserts the line verbatim).
Next session: implement box 1 exactly as the hand-over spells it (the decide() contract, the
site_flow.md paragraph text, the verification list incl. applying decide() to the 21 checkpoint
entries -> exactly badbury confident), then box 2 (site_done wiring), 3 (plan_fit.build how
string) and 4 (tests/test_inkfit.py + the committed seeds; badbury = confident e2e, calleva =
unsure e2e).

# Task 304 survey-fit

## 2026-10-12 304 DECISION: pre-register the survey-sheet fit before any measurement; learned matcher (SuperPoint+LightGlue, pinned) with scale/rotation priors, translation from the matches

Human direction (2026-10-12, iteration 85): (1) dense learned matchers are allowed and
encouraged (RoMa, LoFTR, LightGlue+SuperPoint/DISK, pinned); ask for a GPU via a request if
the 3090 is busy with the model server. (2) Priors that shrink the search: 302b's scale
ratios ran 0.24-13x and rotations to 126 deg; read the plan's north arrow and scale bar
where present, and the site's known size, to fix rotation and scale, leaving mostly
translation. (3) Plan against the survey sheet first; if the matcher also works plan
against aerial, report both columns; same pre-registered held-out protocol; no tuning on
the 21 after the run.

Investigation (all verified on this machine): every one of the 19 held-out sites carries a
georeferenced sheet with coverage=1.0, corners and an _ink.webp - 15 OS six-inch 1st
edition + 1-inch (six-inch = 1:10,560 is the finest), bergues and camp-d-attila Cassini +
Etat-major, aztalan/cahokia/mound-bottom/serpent-mound USGS topo (1:62,500 x3, 1:48,000);
camp-d-attila also has the aerial fr_ortho1950.webp (the aerial column). Plan panels are
on disk (plan_<id>.webp + _ink) for 20 of 21 docs; doc 21 (the DPLA Maiden Castle plan)
has no plan webp - its panel must be cropped from data/raw/commons/big/<id>.jpg (1280x889)
with the doc's crop [165,256,895,663]. Book docs: page at data/raw/books/<ia>/full/<file>
in the doc's size frame + crop (avebury: 3367x5050, crop [700,1000,3150,3700]). 302b seed
records: 8/21 have a scale bar, 12/21 are commons coarse anchors with no scale, 1 has
scale text with unrecorded scan ppi - so the matcher must supply scale for ~13 docs, which
is exactly the learned matcher's job. The catalog's size_m/diameter_m are None for these
sites, so "known size" is available only where a bar or ratio text is recorded.

Machine facts: this VM has no GPU (no /dev/nvidia*, virtual VGA only), 6 cores, 15 GB RAM,
a torch-free .venv; the AI box (10.0.10.75: 3090 Ti = vLLM Qwen model server, 5060 Ti =
clef re-ranker) is not shell-reachable from here (only forced-command keys: doorbell,
labdash-feed, lab-archive; hostname "aibox" does not resolve). A GPU ask_human was drafted
but never sent, because the lead's second message (same iteration) superseded it: a
pre-trained cross-modal matcher, MatchAnything-ELoFTR, is served on the 5060 Ti behind a
VM tunnel at 127.0.0.1:8091 (see the follow-up entry below). .venv is rebuilt from the
hashed requirements.txt before every test run; the matcher runs remotely, so the client
needs only requests + numpy (already in requirements.in) - no requirements change was
made this session.

Action taken: pre-registered in docs/site_flow.md (new section "The one-shot held-out
measurement of the survey-sheet fit (task 304, pre-registered before the run)"): the method
never opens a platefit document (the evaluator fit/surveyfit_heldout.py alone compares
against the hand fits); null recipe = 302b scramble of the panel + 302b half-box shift of
the sheet; score = RANSAC inlier fraction reweighted by reprojection RMS, confidence from
score and margin; the 21 docs = the 302b list unchanged; verdict per the task (a
confidence threshold exists under which every accepted fit is within 60 m and >=5 of 21
are accepted; pass -> 304b wiring, fail -> stop, no fourth method). The section's frozen
config (versions, thresholds, sweep grid, confidence formula) is restated at the freeze
commit, which must precede the run.

Next step (session 2): fit/surveyfit.py v0 against the ELoFTR API (client POST /match with
the plan panel as image0 and the sheet as image1; RANSAC similarity through the matched
pairs; the two nulls; a JSON fit record per call), developed against a stub server (the
real tunnel to 8091 was down at session end - "waiting on the owner, one command") with

## 2026-10-12 304 DECISION: re-read passes on the +theta sign convention (no flip); panel_pieces fixed to mirror template_pieces' piece selection so the scramble draws land exactly as 302b's

Session 3. The file was already committed by the driver (3b4c2b2 "[driver] uncommitted
work after iteration 86"); the import check passed clean (no breakage to fix). The
three Re-read items were re-verified with fresh eyes against the source:

1. **Sign convention (CRITICAL item) - passes, no flip.**
   `propose._anchor_pairs` is `z = (m_per_px/k) * e^{i theta} * (w - wc) + zc` with
   `w = u - i v` (page coords, v down) and `z` Mercator (imag = north). A visual
   (on-screen) counterclockwise rotation by phi multiplies the `w = x - i y` frame by
   **+e^{i phi}** (verified: at 90 deg the up vector (0,-1) goes to left (-1,0), which is
   CCW on screen and +i in the w frame). A drawn plan whose north arrow is theta deg
   CLOCKWISE of page-up has the 302b anchor factor e^{i theta}; rotating the panel image
   by **+theta** (PIL `rotate(+deg)` is counterclockwise on screen) brings it north-up.
   So `_match_theta`'s `rotate_image(panel, +theta)` and `rotated_panel_map`'s
   `R = [[c, s], [-s, c]]` (the visual-CCW matrix in x-right/y-down) are both correct,
   and the composition `a_s * a_r * e^{i theta} * fs` is exactly the anchor form
   `(m/k) e^{i theta}`. The synthetic test must still lock this (see hand-over).
2. **Frame separation in `composite_placement` - passes.** The similarity (a, b) is fit
   on the four FULL-FRAME (doc size frame) corners; the reported panel corners are the
   CROP-box corners (size units, `_panel_crop`: map_box fractions * size, or (0,0,w,h)
   full frame) pushed through that same similarity. The composition of the three
   stage maps is an exact similarity in the w frame, so the full-frame fit is valid at
   the crop corners. `fit()`'s `size = row.size or [panel.width, panel.height]` matches
   `inkfit._size_frame`.
3. **Null phase - passes.** Both null `_match_theta` calls pass the UNROTATED panel
   (real or scrambled) plus the chosen theta; the rotation is applied inside, exactly
   as in the fit phase. Scramble recipe checked against 302b: same seed (20261012 =
   `inkfit.NULL_SEED`), same per-piece draw order (th, dx, dy), same R (`_rot2` form),
   clip, white background; the sheet shift matches 302b's DEM null
   (`ndi.shift(..., (-h/2, +w/2), order=0)`, cval 255 instead of NaN for an image).

**One real bug found and fixed (committed):** `panel_pieces` kept every autowalls keep
label, but the 302b reference `inkfit.template_pieces` drops labels that have zero of
`extract`'s 1-in-step sampled points (deterministic seed-0 subsample, step=2). A label
that 302b drops would still consume (th, dx, dy) draws from the single seeded RNG,
shifting every later piece's transform in the scrambled null image. Fix: select labels
through the sampled points, exactly as `template_pieces` does (`comp = lab[pts...]`;
a piece exists iff a sampled point hits its label). Verified on a synthetic panel:
identical (idx, length) piece lists, identical first scramble draw, valid scrambled
image; `ransac` recovers an exact planted similarity to 1e-14; `sheet_similarity`
returns |a| = 10, rot 0 deg for a known 10-merc-m/px sheet. Suite green: 68 passed,
1 skipped (the earlier known reds from other tasks are resolved on main; nothing mine
touches them). Matcher still live: /health -> zju-community/matchanything_eloftr on the
RTX 5060 Ti.

Next (session 4): write tests/test_surveyfit.py - the hermetic stub-server suite
(details in the task hand-over), then the real-service smoke, freeze, held-out
evaluator, one run.

### 2026-10-12 304 DECISION: hermetic stub-server test suite for the survey fit (tests/test_surveyfit.py written, not yet run); sign convention verified by code

Session 4. Wrote the complete tests/test_surveyfit.py (7 tests, uncommitted on disk -
harness hand-over). Design decisions, recorded for the next agent:

1. **Sign convention verified by code (closes the session-3 "must still lock" item).**
   Ran a probe: PIL rotate(+90) sends a marker right-of-centre to up-of-centre (visual
   CCW, matches the docs); rotated_panel_map(90) sends the same point to the same place
   (its R = [[c,s],[-s,c]] is the visual-CCW matrix in x-right/y-down); and in the
   w = x - i y frame a visual CCW rotation by phi sends a point's coordinate to
   e^{+i phi} * w (checked numerically for both basis vectors). My own mid-session hand
   derivation that got e^{-i phi} was an arithmetic slip (mis-decoding w = x - iy for
   "up"): the module's convention and docstring are correct. Corollary (derived, to be
   confirmed by the recovery tests): the true pairs satisfy a_r = (a_true/a_s) e^{-i
   theta_sent} and the module's composition a_s*a_r*e^{i theta} recovers a_true for ANY
   theta_sent - the rotation is absorbed by the fit, so the sweep discriminates only
   through matcher match quality (the stub simulates this with a degradation rule).

2. **The stub matcher (port 0, never a fixed port - parallel checkouts).** It decodes
   the two sent PNGs and simulates the service: sheet classified by 3x3 ink windows at
   the world's sheet centres (real sheet >= 12 of 14 seen; the 302b half-box shift ==
   all 8 surviving SW-quadrant glyphs seen at (x+200, y-200)); the plan image's
   rotation resolved to 2 deg from connected-component centroids matched against 180
   candidate expected positions (hit window 2.0 px; oriented iff >= 8 of 14 hit - plan
   centre radii 43-200 px make one fine step's 2.8-7 px displacement a clean miss);
   true pairs returned in the ORIGINAL (sent) pixel frames as the service does,
   degraded by the angular error vs the drawing's own rotation (<=3 deg: all
   survivors; <=15: first 6; else none) so the frozen sweep has something to score;
   plus one fixed 12-pair junk set (seed 304777).

3. **The world (all constants in the test file; measured outcomes go to
   data/surveyfit/devlog.md):** site (2.0, 50.0), 2000 m box (square in Mercator, so
   the sheet's a_s is exactly real ~7.7786), 400x400 sheet, 14 glyph discs (7x2 grid,
   x=30+50i, y in {250,310}), 320x320 plan at M_PX = 4.0 (|a_true|/|a_s| = 0.8; plan
   glyph radius 4.375 px; drawn at 4x supersampling for sub-pixel centres; saved as
   JPEG q95 to stand in for a scanned picture), b_true chosen so the plan centre
   lands on the site centre, 4 junk glyphs at the corners (>= 25 px from true ones;
   a rigid rotation preserves the gap, so they never match at any candidate theta),
   12 junk match pairs (seed 304777). Three drawing rotations exercised: 42 (fixed
   rot), 0 (sweep; the fine grid wraps 348..12 and the 0/12 deg images are sent
   twice - the re-read item from session 3 is now a test assertion, not a note), 6
   (sweep refinement between coarse values).

4. **Expected score order (to be confirmed, then logged):** 14-true ~0.53 > 6-true
   ~0.30 > junk-only ~0.19; the shifted-sheet null carries 8 true pairs ~0.34 < fit.
   If the junk-only RANSAC model ever scores above the 6-true level, the theta_d=6
   sweep's argmax can break - the fix is a different JUNK_SEED (304778, ...), not a
   change to the frozen module.

5. **Fixture (test 6, the test_gate pattern):** data/surveyfit/fixture_match.json is
   generated by `.venv/bin/python tests/test_surveyfit.py --emit-fixture` (once, then
   committed): the ideal true pairs of the theta_d=42 world + the junk set, with the

### 2026-10-12 304 DECISION: first real run of the stub suite - the synthetic world was mis-scaled and is now centred at (195,235) with 25/30 px pitch; a match-quality jitter (0.6 px/deg) makes the true rotation the unique sweep max; scipy 1.18.1 changed center_of_mass's array-index return

Session 5, first execution of tests/test_surveyfit.py. Three distinct findings, in order:

1. **The session-4 world did not fit its own frame.** The old 7x2 grid (x=30+50i, y in
   {250,310}) is 300 sheet-px wide = 375 plan-px at the plan's 0.8x scale - wider than the
   320 px plan frame - and its centre (180,280) is 100 m west / 400 m south of the sheet
   centre, so in the plan frame (plan centre on the site centre) the glyph field sat
   off-centre: one true glyph landed at x=-67.5 (off-frame) and another within 9 px of the
   (15,305) junk corner at theta_d=0. The junk-gap assertion caught it on the very first run.
   Redesign constraint (verified numerically, not by eye): a 7-col grid can have exactly 4
   columns <= 198 and 2 rows >= 202 (the 302b half-box shift keeps 8 glyphs) ONLY when the
   grid centre is off-centre (pitch (198-c)/2 < p_x <= 198-c), while the plan frame (scale
   0.8, centre on site) needs the grid within ~155 px of the panel centre at every
   theta_d in {0,6,42}. Chosen: pitch 25/30 px, centre (195,235) - cols 120..270, rows
   220/250. Consequences (all measured): 8 shift survivors; all 14 glyphs in-frame at all
   three theta_d; min junk gap 35-93 px; and crucially the stub's rotation resolution is
   binary at the grid's 2 deg step (true theta: 14 hits; any 2-deg-off candidate: 4 hits),
   so the sent rotation always resolves to itself, never to a neighbour.

2. **Score ties, broken by a quality wobble, not a count cut.** With the binary hit gate,
   a call one fine step (2 deg) away from the true rotation still resolves and returns all
   14 true pairs (delta <= 3 band), so without further degradation it would TIE the true
   rotation's score and the sweep's first-max rule could land on 358/2 instead of 0. Fix
   (test-side only, no frozen-module change): the stub's returned plan-side points wobble
   by up to 0.6 px per degree of angular error, seeded per (theta, i) - deterministic and
   repeat-call-stable, mimicking a matcher whose match QUALITY degrades with rotation. The
   resulting score landscape is strictly ordered: delta 0 ~0.52 > delta 2 ~0.48 >> delta
   4-15 ~0.15-0.19 ~ junk-only ~0.15. The sweep is now deterministic: theta_d=0 -> 0, theta
   _d=6 -> 6, unique maxima in both cases (the fine-0 duplicate payload ties the coarse 0
   and the strict > keeps the first).

3. **Two crashes, both test-side.** (a) The anticipated zero-centroid ValueError in
   _resolve_theta (empty scrambled image): guarded. (b) NEW, unanticipated: scipy 1.18.1's
   ndi.center_of_mass(input, labels, index_array) returns a LIST OF (cy, cx) TUPLES (one
   per label), not a (cy-array, cx-array) pair - `cy, cx = ...` raises "too many values to
   unpack (expected 2)" whenever n != 2. _centroids rewritten to consume the list form. The
   single-mask form ndi.center_of_mass(mask) still returns a (cy, cx) tuple, so
   fit/surveyfit.py's panel_pieces (line 344) is unaffected - verified. (PITFALLS candidate.
   Note: the .venv the driver builds from requirements.txt pins this scipy; the code must
   match ITS behaviour, not an older one.)

After these fixes the suite runs end to end against the stub; first failure is now a
tolerance, not a crash: test 1 (theta_d=42, rot=42, no null) reports the placement centre
2.958 m from the independently constructed true placement (limit 2 m). Diagnosis (measured,
throwaway script): my 4x-supersample rasterizer (4x4 cells, acc >= 0.3) biases each disc's
measured centroid 0.44-0.66 px from the intended sub-pixel centre before any JPEG, and
0.34-0.77 px after the module's 42 deg PIL bicubic rotation - the 16-cell coverage
quantisation makes the effective ink boundary asymmetric, and the 14 per-glyph biases share
a common component (fitted b off ~0.7 plan-px = 2.9 m; da and the corners were not yet
reached by the -x run). Hypothesis: 8x supersampling (1/64 coverage resolution) cuts the
bias to ~0.1 px; the JPEG q95 roundtrip (what the stub actually decodes) adds at most a
~0.1 px symmetric blur. One open question from the last experiment: a single-disc 8x test
case came back with ZERO ink components after the JPEG roundtrip (the script crashed on
the empty array before printing which point) - if a 7-px disc genuinely vanishes at q95,
enlarge the world's glyph radii (SHEET_R 3.5 -> 5) rather than the encoder. Tolerances
will be set to ~2x the observed worst case and the measured numbers logged in the devlog,
as pre-decided in session 4.

Next (session 6): 8x supersample (verify the vanishing-disc question), rerun, recalibrate

## 2026-10-12 304 DECISION: 8x supersampling fails (the disc-centroid bias is a threshold-edge effect, and 8x is worse); the stub now returns sub-pixel keypoints at the expected glyph positions (+-0.25 px wobble + the 0.6 px/deg jitter), keeping the measured centroids for glyph DETECTION only; fixed a real ndi.shift rank bug in shift_sheet_image; stub suite 7/7 green, fixture committed, tolerances tightened to ~2x observed; the real ELoFTR service now answers at 8091

Session 6. (1) The 8x supersample experiment (throwaway, then deleted) DISCONFIRMS the
session-5 hypothesis: the per-disc centroid bias is NOT 4x quantisation. Measured (full test
path: rasterise -> lossy save -> [plan] PIL bicubic rotate -> connected-component centroids
vs the expected positions): r = 3.5 discs - 4x: 0.33-0.68 px, 8x: 0.43-0.76 px (WORSE); r = 5
- 4x: 0.37-0.70 px, 8x: 0.49-0.88 px. Even INTEGER-centred discs (sheet) are biased: 0.329 px
(4x) / 0.707 px (8x) - the 0.3 coverage threshold cuts the small-disc edge asymmetrically
about the sub-pixel centre, and finer supersampling only changes which edge pixels land
inside. JPEG q95 (the plan's path) and WEBP q95 (the sheet's) add at most ~0.05 px, and NO
disc vanishes in the 14-disc world (the session-5 single-disc vanishing did not reproduce).
(2) Design consequence (the fix this session, tests/test_surveyfit.py only): the stub's
connected-component centroids are a poor model of a real matcher's KEYPOINT ACCURACY (a
trained sub-pixel regressor, not a thresholded-blob centroid), so the stub now returns, for
each detected glyph, its EXPECTED sub-pixel position in the sent frame (the module's own
visual-rotation matrix) plus a small deterministic per-(theta, i) matcher wobble (+-0.25 px,
seeded, a stream separate from the quality jitter) plus the existing 0.6 px/deg angular
jitter. The sent-image geometry still drives what the stub sees: glyph DETECTION (which
glyph, which rotation, real vs shifted sheet) still runs on the measured centroids, and the
_take bands (all 14 / first 6 / none), the junk set, and the response format are unchanged.
This is a more faithful matcher model, not a weaker test: the placement tolerance now means
"what a good sub-pixel matcher's fit would produce", and the sweep still scores rotation
through quality degradation. A throwaway-script red herring consumed some time first: my
8x prototype rasteriser had INVERTED polarity (white ink / black paper) and looked like a
single mega-blob - the module's `_rasterize` convention is black = ink (`255 * (~ink)`);
checked rather than believed. (3) REAL MODULE BUG found and fixed (fit/surveyfit.py,
shift_sheet_image): `ndi.shift` on the RGB sheet (rank 3) with a 2-element shift sequence
raises "RuntimeError: sequence argument must have length equal to input rank" - the null
path had never executed before (tests 1-3 run null=False). Fixed with a 3-element shift
(0.0 on the channel axis). (4) Tolerances tightened to ~2x the OBSERVED worst (the
recorded-pin discipline): observed worst over the four worlds - db 0.624 m (theta_d=42),
da 0.00273 m/px, panel corners 0.797 m; null run (theta_d=0): fit 0.5278 vs null 0.3923,
margin 0.1355, confidence 0.0715. New asserts: db < 1.3, da < 0.006, corners < 1.6. (5)
Fixture emitted and committed: data/surveyfit/fixture_match.json (north-42 ideal world, 26
pairs, 14 inliers, rms 3.6e-12, score 0.538462) - test 6 (reproduce exactly) passes.
(6) Full suite: 75 passed, 1 skipped (was 68 passed 1 skipped before my 7 tests).
(7) The REAL service now answers: GET 127.0.0.1:8091/health ->
{"model": "zju-community/matchanything_eloftr", "device": "NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5060 Ti"}.

drawing landed on the map (depictions poll passed) but the 30 s drain for "finished (exit 0)" timed
out with only the PUT log line visible. Investigation (throwaway /tmp/diag305*.py, raw os.read
reader + watchdog dumps): NO hang - the whole one-site line finishes in 23 s (PUT at +1 s, first
worker print and "finished (exit 0)" at +23 s; the ~22 s is the heavy first import of the
numpy/scipy/PIL chain). The drain pattern was the bug: select() on the fd of a buffered reader
(proc.stdout, text mode) is not a wait for lines - the first readline() pre-fetched the OS pipe
into Python's buffer and select never fired again; a second run also showed a whole-process stall
of minutes (its watchdog time.sleep(60) never woke - an OS-level thing under parallel-agent load,
not a Python deadlock: the identical run completes in 23 s elsewhere). Fix in tests/test_fit_queue.py:
the server's stdout is pulled by a dedicated reader thread (blocking reads, no select at all), the
fit-entry poll and the "finished" wait merged into one 180 s loop (8x the measured 23 s; the server-
stays-up assert runs every tick), and the network-freedom / no-failure asserts scan the collected
lines. Part 1 (synthetic + real population) unchanged and green. Full suite after: 71 passed,
1 skipped (baseline 70+1; the +1 is part 2). web/data/fit_queue.json re-generated: content identical
(117 entries, same order - the batch docs are agent docs, which do not move a site out of the queue),
only the "at" stamp moved; committed. PITFALLS.md: new Python entry (select + buffered reader on a
coprocess pipe), shared-store entry corrected (382 symlinks + 6 tracked hand-fit dirs + the untracked
real shotwick dir). Next: the headless card probe with screenshots (box 4) and the docs/site_flow.md
section (box 1's "say how" + the card/save->line semantics).

- FAILED (twice, same root causes, both fixed): (1) shot.mjs's `page.evaluate(js)` with `js =
  'async () => {...}'` returned undefined - Playwright 1.63 treats the string as an expression and does

  throwaway probe page). (2) `window.S` is undefined in page context - S is a top-level const (page-wide
  lexical environment, not globalThis); fix: bare `S` with a typeof guard. Both are now PITFALLS
  candidates (filed in the 306a hand-over; the context-limit message barred editing PITFALLS.md).

## 2026-10-12 306a DECISION: shotwick removed (2x too big, proven from the plate + NHLE listing); rebuild via upsert_item + doc delete + plan_fit.prune + review.export instead of tools/site_done.py because the matcher is reachable and its fit phase would place a NEW unaudited fit
- Verdict: WRONG SIZE. The 1897 plate's moated ring fills the whole 839x840 plate (ink bbox 822 x 822
  px measured with PIL) and the fitted 0.2289 m/px maps it to 188 m x 188 m on the ground; the real
  earthworks are at most ~90 m across (40 m x 20 m hexagonal motte, 25 m-wide surrounding ditch,
  38 m bailey - the NHLE 1016616 listing description, read through web.archive.org because
  historicengland and heritagegateway both 403 from this VM). The 4 turret anchors' 2-19 m residuals
  are the ~2x scale mismatch the fit_gate cannot reject at n=3-6 (the documented blind spot). The
  aerial (even zoomed to the page's cap of 18) shows only lawn where the ring sits: the earthworks
  are buried, so the size proof is document + listing, not the picture.
- Removal mechanics (all the product's own calls): review.upsert_item('.', sid, [entry]) with the
  reason in `why` (entry: plan/title/url/pairs - same shape as site_done._pattern_entry; the item's
  lead stayed earthworkenglan01allcgoog_n0575_1, so the file did not move), delete the doc,
  plan_fit.prune('.', sid) (drops the overlay + webp, refreshes drawings via build_site.set_drawings),
  review.export('.', site_id=sid) (page re-published, item open).
- FAILED (a design trap, not an approach failure): running `tools/site_done.py
  shotwick-castle-q7502621` as the task text suggested would have re-fired the FIT phase -
  matchfit.reachable() is True (127.0.0.1 matcher health probe answers) and the earthwork row has
  no doc, so the matcher would have placed a NEW agent fit on the map during an audit session -
  exactly what 306 ("none reaches the map unseen") is there to prevent. site_done only prunes when
  its own sync sends a pattern doc, so even the deletion would not have been covered by it. The
  4-call sequence above produces the identical outcome (overlay gone, count refreshed, item
  re-exported) without the fit phase. If site_done ever gets a `--no-fit` flag, this note can be
  dropped.

## 2026-10-12 306a DECISION: the bethlehem-q2523914 site is the DUTCH Bethlehem (Het Hogeland, Groningen) - its 7 SoP sheets are a toponymic collision and its 2 basilica plans are building interiors fitted as ground maps, pending confirmation
- First bethlehem aerial actually read (c_a164, the 1748 "Plan de Maestricht et environs, detail
  Severen & Kanjel"): page header says "BETHLEHEM / Het Hogeland, Groningen, Netherlands" over a
  Dutch polder, and the drawing's ink reads Severen / Cantel / C. Harnesstg - all real Het Hogeland
  Dutch polder, and the drawing's ink reads Severen / Cantel / C. Harnesstg - all real Het Hogeland
  hamlets. So the 3 Maestricht-1748 details (c_a164, c_a42a, c_d624) look like LOCAL maps of the
  right place (c_a164 aligned on the polder: Severen hamlet, village centre, field boundaries),
  while 9 of the 12 are not: the 7 "Survey of Palestine, Bethlehem 01/03/05/06/08/09/11" ground
  sheets are maps of JUDEAN Bethlehem fitted to a site ~2000 km away (near-zero residuals measure
  only the local pattern match - sheet grid vs polder grid - the wrong-PLACE class of the shotwick
  size error), and the 2 basilica plans (c_67ed Sandys 1615 "Plan of the Basilica of the Nativity",
  c_7ee7 "(1881) PLAN SOUTERRAINS DE LA BASILIQUE") are BUILDING INTERIOR plans (one rotated
  -18.4 deg) fitted as if they were ground maps - a third failure class (wrong KIND) that applies
  regardless of which Bethlehem the site is. Session cut at the context limit before confirming
  Q2523914, the site.json place field, or the pairs' lon/lat cluster (Groningen ~53.3N/6.3E vs
  Judea ~31.7N/35.2E settles which side of the collision holds for the 7 SoP sheets). No bethlehem
  line is written in site_flow.md until this is settled; no bethlehem doc touched.

  zoom before startFit and re-issue the easeTo with the explicit target zoom after refit.

  scale): measure the plate's OWN scale bar (PIL: crop the plate's lower region 3x and read it -
  worked for lemanis/n0110_1/cawthorne); a doc whose fitted mpx matches its scale bar/stated scale
  within ~20% is trustworthy, and a gate rejection of "stated X disagrees with its own points (Y)"
  means the doc's near-stated fit is the one to believe. Also: red-ink 6-inch/1-inch/1848 maps are
  georeferenced ground truth - anchors on same-named red-ink labels = placement verified. Coarse
  item candidates store only 3-6 rectangle points (feature names live in `why`), so compare
  centers/scales, not feature positions.

  d'Orléans en 1828'. Discovery: the site's canonical point (1.909, 47.9025, Wikidata Q2710144
  'Cenabum') IS the city of Orléans (1.9°E, 47.9°N) - I had mis-remembered Orléans at 2.09°E and
  nearly called it a 12 km miss. Verification: the 1850s IGN état-major (GEOF, layer etatmajor)
  tiles show 'ORLÉANS' printed at that point (radial old town, fortified quays, bridges; the
  (2.09, 47.90) area is open Mardre/Donnery country with no town). The drawn 1828 city aligns
  with that georeferenced survey within 100-200 m (radial centre, Faubourg St-Vincent, river,
  bridges), and the fitted 5.41 m/px equals the plate's ~1:20000 printed scale at scan dpi (the
  scan has no scale bar; least-squares transform from the 8 anchors reproduces the stored fit:
  5.389 m/px, +1.06°). The item's 'Epinal' Q173695 (~350 km NE) 200-sheet evidence = sheet-level
  red herring (same pattern as argentoratum c_e882645's 'Ville' hit).
- ferme-d-azincourt c_804aa7: the plate is the 1810 cadastre of the Ferme d'Azincourt hamlet
  ('Monts d'Azincourt') of the Émerchicourt commune (Nord) - this site itself, not the Agincourt
  village of the Somme. The plate's own road labels ('Chemin d'Émerchicourt', 'Chemin de
  Boursolain', 'Bonnay') proved it; Nominatim reverse-geocode of anchor 1 = 'Rue d'Azincourt,
  Émerchicourt'; the drawn river = the real Scarpe near Aniche. Red farm cluster on the real
  hamlet, roads on roads (aerial + relief). Fitted 1.23 m/px agrees with the item's own points
  (1.171), its stated 0.55 does not. Lesson: when a plate's title names a famous place, check
  the plate's OWN toponyms before trusting the title (names are recycled - farms/streets are
  named after famous battles).
- dudley c_e66ec: 1885 'Plan of Dudley Castle Grounds' on the real conical Castle Rock (summit
  anchor on the real summit, aerial + relief; 2639/4998 inliers vs the 1842 OS 6-inch first

  mounds maps trace the real Gentry Mound / S-ring / Watson Bayou on the LiDAR relief (the

  Swol river on the real meanders (aerial + relief; 1981/4998 vs tmk1850).

# Task 307a place-check

## 2026-10-12 307a DECISION: iteration 108 research, written late (iteration 109) - the place-check rule set and the page-work anchors
The iteration-108 session investigated the original 307a (the namesake place check) and
was re-tasked mid-session by the lead (box 1 -> 309 for agent c; agent a -> the page
work). It wrote the re-split record and the full 309 spec but never wrote the DECISIONS
entry its hand-over promised; this entry is that write-up, summarising with pointers
rather than restating (the detail lives in tasks/309-place-check.md, kept as reference,
and in the parent 307's Hand-over):
- Place-check rule set: decide from KNOWN places only - a mismatch only when a known
  caption place contradicts the site's (a different county within one country, a
different country, or resolved coordinates far outside the site's region); an unknown
place never mismatches (fail open - necessary because 52 queue candidates carry noisy
recorded conflicts that are mostly title words, not places); a place that contains the
site (a country-level word, the site's own county/name) is not a mismatch; re-verify
recorded verdicts offline (the evidence-check pattern: a recorded mismatch whose stored
coordinates no longer contradict is flagged, not silently kept).
- Measured cases: n0686 "The Devil's Dyke Camp, near Brighton" on the Cambridgeshire
dyke - the right site exists (devil-s-dyke-hillfort-q17674160, Poynings, West Sussex,
unbuilt) -> move there, not review; n0354 "ARDOCH, PERTHSHIRE." on the Spanish
Coria/Vindomora - the catalog (12,603 rows, name+aliases) has no Ardoch -> review with
the reason. Queue = 117 entries.
- Page-work anchors (307b): propose_for(n0686) -> scale_m_px 0.3621, 5 pairs centred on
the site, coarse false; refit() maps P.inkUrl onto the 'fit-prev' image source from the
least-squares similarity of P.pairs, so "the drawing starts on the map" = seed P.pairs
with the proposal's pairs, then handlers edit the anchor (drag/scale/rotate) and re-
emit pairs; the saved doc stays pairs-shaped, so plan_fit.py/the gate/the one-site line
are untouched.

# Task 307bb move-it

## 2026-10-12 307bb DECISION: the placement gestures live in fitkit.js by reassigning refit (no index.html footprint, no new map listeners) - designed in full, not yet coded
Investigation-only session: 307ba is done (suite 75+1, re-claimed at c5250a4 sync), so
307bb's start condition is met. No code written; the full implementation design (function
by function) is in 307bb's hand-over. Key decisions, all verified against the sources:

- **MapLibre 5.6.0 binds ALL gesture listeners on the canvas container, not the canvas**
  (web/vendor/maplibre-gl-5.6.0.js, readable UMD: mousedown/mousemove/mouseup/dblclick/
  click/wheel{passive:false} on getCanvasContainer(), mousemove{capture}/mouseup on
  document). Consequence: fitkit's gesture listeners go on the container; with
  dragPan/scrollZoom/touchZoomRotate/doubleClickZoom/boxZoom disabled while placed, the
  dispatcher (`if(!c.isEnabled())continue;`) skips them and none preventDefaults (the
  scrollZoom handler returns before its e.preventDefault() when disabled) - a
  later-registered container listener receives the events, so wheel/drag can own the mouse
  without fighting the map.
- **The 3px/4px disjointness is real, not assumed:** the always-enabled user-event handler
  records _mousedownPos on every mousedown and fires the map 'click' only when the
  mousedown->click distance is < clickTolerance (3 px, the page's default). Our move
  engages at >4 px, so a pinning click and a moving drag cannot both fire; we null
  P.pending the moment a move engages (a drag is not a pin).
- **Anchor refinement hooks through a wrapped refit, not a map listener:** the page's own
  map-click (pin), undo, clear and marker-dragend handlers all call the bare refit() - the
  global - so `refit = wrapper` sees every pair-count change: n grew -> push the pre-pin
  anchor(s) on P.anchorStack, refine the anchor by simFit over all pairs (view mode too:
  the anchor stays a similarity; verified algebraically simFit's a,b equal the re-emitted
  similarity's a,z0, so over the exact five it recovers the anchor to fp rounding),
  re-emit the five in place (mutate lon/lat, keep the objects - the marker dragend
  closures hold them), rebuild markers via the page's addPairMarker (push one at a time
  for 1..n labels); n shrank -> pop the stack (clear -> the anchor before the first pin;
  undo below the five-re-emit floor with an empty stack leaves the anchor untouched: the
  placement is inviolable). A drag gesture does NOT absorb the fit first (a move must not
  change mpx/rot); wheel/rotate do (so a previously dragged corner is absorbed into the
  anchor before the transform).
- **Gestures transform the anchor:** drag = centre += ground delta (unproject at both
  pointer points, exact at any zoom, fly-to-tolerant); wheel = mpx *= exp(-deltaY*0.0015)
  (the page canvas's own factor: wheel-up grows); shift+wheel = rot += deltaY*0.01 (1 deg
  per 100-unit notch). Applied rAF-coalesced (headless rAF works on this VM - the 307ba
  probe's fly-to animations ran). The page's fit pane already shows the fit's mpx/rot;
  fitkit additionally overwrites #fithint after each refit with the live anchor readout +
  gesture legend (box 1's "live scale and rotation in the panel").
- **Save needs no new code:** with P.pairs = re-emitted five (+ pins), the page's saveFit
  already writes exactly the doc the box asks for (same page-px frame: 307ba verified
  every current queue entry's served image IS its recorded size, so the row-size frame is
  the image frame). The fitqueue wrapper marks the site done; the one-site line runs as in
  part 2. The headless proof (box 3) pins all of it in pixels.
- **Deactivate placement at the TOP of the openPlan wrapper** (before the inner chain):
  review.js's wrapper pushes RV.pending pairs inside the chain and "Another plan" is
  endFit(false)->startFit->openPlan - a post-chain clear would let either inherit the
  previous placement; the 404-page path (part 3's served tree) still returns at the first
