## 2026-10-12 307bb DECISION: iteration 124's committed gestures verified in a throwaway headless probe - all placement state green, the DRAG gesture is broken (LngLat object into the array-destructuring merc -> NaN); next session fixes the drag and re-runs
Verification-only session (the gesture code was written in iteration 124 and driver-committed as 711bc01 without verification; fitkit md5 5b94a8b081a481ea74925f5770f5214d). Built the iter-123 hand-over's recipe as a throwaway at /tmp/fkverify/: build_tree.py (line_served-style FULL tree: web+server+tools+fit+pipeline, data minus data/raw, Devil's Dyke's page n0686.jpg materialised into the tree's own data/raw, server via the GUARD with every socket dead, --port 0) and probe.mjs (headless Chrome 1200x800: card -> #fqplace -> wait P.placed && P.img -> seed assertions -> drag / wheel / shift+wheel / pin / undo / #fsave -> poll tree doc + overlay -> fresh run with aw.fitQueue.done='{}' + aw.depSel seeded -> #fcancel restore).

**Verified GOOD (all green in pixels):** place mode opens from the card with the anchor EXACTLY the entry's propose (mpx 0.3621, rot 0, centre 0.297247,52.269819 = the propose's centre pair - derived from the served queue file, never pinned); 5 re-emitted pairs + 5 draggable markers; ALL FIVE map handlers disabled while placed (dragPan, scrollZoom, touchZoomRotate, doubleClickZoom, boxZoom); the site's seeded dep layer hidden; fit-prev source up; the panel hint live ("0.36 m per page pixel, turned 0.0° · drag to move · wheel to scale · shift+wheel to turn"). No pageerror before the crash.

**The bug caught (why the rest of the probe never ran):** web/index.html:342-343 defines `merc = ([lon, lat]) => ...` / `unmerc = ([x, y]) => ...` - they destructure ARRAYS. fitkit's `_fkOnDown` stores `ll: map.unproject(pos)` and `_fkOnMove` computes `b = merc(map.unproject([x, y]))`, but MapLibre 5.6's unproject returns a LngLat OBJECT {lng, lat} - array destructuring of an object yields undefined -> the anchor's lon/lat become NaN on the first drag move (every re-emit after is NaN; the wheel's _fkAbsorb would then poison scale/rotation too). The iter-123 hand-over's design (item 11) contained the same latent bug (`ll: map.unproject(pos)`), so iteration 124's code faithfully reproduces it - this is a DESIGN bug, not a transcription slip. The fix (next session, before any further gesture work): in `_fkOnDown` store `ll: [u.lng, u.lat]` and in `_fkOnMove` compute `b = merc([u.lng, u.lat])` from the unproject result - a one-line-class change, no restructure; then re-run the whole probe end-to-end (the drag/wheel/turn/pin/undo/save/cancel assertions have NOT yet been observed passing).

**Process notes for the probe (all tripped this session, all fixed in /tmp/fkverify/probe.mjs):** node single-quoted strings must not contain apostrophes (two SyntaxError crashes); playwright-core's page.evaluate takes at most ONE argument (wrap extras in an object); page globals `merc`/`unmerc` exist but `project`/`unproject` are map methods; the run-2 cancel check must re-seed aw.fitQueue.done='{}' (the run-1 save marks the site done in browser localStorage and moves the card on); the probe's own in-page drag-expectation formula must convert LngLat to [lon,lat] before merc (it crashed on that line). Suite for the post-fix session: `/home/agent/projects/ancient-works/.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ -q` (sibling venv, requirements.txt byte-identical; our checkout has no .venv yet) - baseline 75 passed + 1 skipped, 0 failed.

### 2026-10-12 307bb DECISION: iteration 127 - the probe re-run: all 33 gesture/pin/undo/save-doc checks GREEN; the save mystery solved (the GUARD's dead pipe, not the page) and three probe bugs fixed; remaining: run the server under the venv python (site_done needs numpy), fix the depSel seed shape, allow Esri in the reqfail filter
Ran the iter-126 next step: killed the stale tree server, rebuilt (the fixed fitkit lands in the tree), re-ran the throwaway probe. Run 127 (pre build_tree fix) got 33 [ok] - all seed, drag (the 1.5x bug is gone), wheel, shift+wheel (the rotated-parallelogram claim verified in pixels), pin, undo - then failed at save (S.db null) and crashed on its own `site is not defined` in the run-2 cancel evaluate.

**The save mystery, solved (the biggest finding):** build_tree.py read the GUARD server's stdout pipe only up to the URL line and then EXITS - closing the pipe's read end. Every subsequent `sys.stderr.write` in the server raises BrokenPipeError. serve.py's `log_message` writes on EVERY /api/ request and every non-200 status, and BaseHTTPRequestHandler calls `log_request` INSIDE `send_response` - before the buffered status line is flushed by end_headers. So with the dead pipe, every /api/ response (including /api/db/ping, the page's db handshake) and every 404 died mid-response: the browser saw ERR_EMPTY_RESPONSE, the page's openDb (index.html ~1499) returned null, S.db stayed null, and saveFit returned silently at its first line (`if (!S.db || ...) return;`) - status text untouched, no pageerror, no PUT. 200-static files skip the log and work, which is why the page loaded, the book page loaded, and all placement checks passed. Proven live: with the pipe dead, curl showed /api/db/ping and non-existent /data/ files = "Empty reply from server", while /data/fit_queue.json = 200 OK; the server process had 1 thread (all handler threads dead). Fix (in /tmp/fkverify/build_tree.py only): the server's stdout/stderr go to a FILE (tree.log) - a file has no read end, so nothing blocks, dies, or fills. After the fix, run 127b: ping OK, db:true, the page's own PUT landed, and the doc was written to the tree store with by:hand, size = the loaded image, and pairs EXACTLY the final re-emitted five (the page's own rounding) - box 2's core proven end-to-end in the browser. The committed line_served fixture is unaffected (pytest keeps a reader thread on the pipe for the test's lifetime, and the test PUTs from the test process, never through the page).

**Three probe bugs fixed in /tmp/fkverify/probe.mjs (none are page bugs):**
1. "pin: the anchor was refined" read `pinOut.u0`/`pinOut.v0` - the keys don't exist (u0/v0 are under `pinOut.a`) - `undefined === 1683.5` failed the check though the anchor had refined correctly (the before/after dump proved it: lon 0.2994483->0.2997432, mpx 0.420531->0.420441, rot 1->0.999864). Fixed to read under `.a`.
2. "pin: the sixth pair is the click" expected 1e-7 equality between the pinned pair's lon/lat and unproject of the requested pixel - but the browser QUANTIZES CDP mouse points to whole pixels before the DOM (measured across diag3-6: requested (1004.891, 385.000) -> clientX 1004, offsetX 1005; (1004.886, 385.000) -> the MapLibre event point (1004, 385), a 0.89px/0.001px shift, transform provably static). MapLibre 5.6's MapMouseEvent computes `point = n.mousePos(canvas, e) = (clientX - rect.left)/scale - clientLeft` (vendor getScale/getPoint) from that quantized DOM event, so the page's pin pair is the unproject of an integer pixel ~1px from my float. A physical mouse is quantized identically, the doc rounds to 7dp at save, and the fit is a least squares over several points - so this is not a product defect. The check now bounds the difference by 2x the per-pixel lng/lat step (measured in-page from two unprojects 2px apart). The committed part-4 test must encode the same tolerance, not exact equality.
3. The run-2 cancelOut evaluate referenced `site` (a probe-local) inside the page context -> ReferenceError. Fixed by passing `{site}` as the single evaluate argument.

**Remaining failures in run 127b (three, all diagnosed, none yet fixed):** (a) the one-site line no-opped: tree.log says `one-site line for <site>: unavailable in this tree (ModuleNotFoundError("No module named 'numpy'"))` - build_tree.py spawned the server with SYSTEM python3; tools/site_done.py needs numpy. Fix: Popen the server with the venv python (our checkout's .venv if present, else the sibling /home/agent/projects/ancient-works/.venv - requirements.txt byte-identical). Consequence: the page's 90s poll times out, leaves the pane open, so "page closed the fit view" and "placement state cleared" fail as knock-ons. (b) the depSel localStorage seed `{ [site]: 'os1inch.webp' }` did not take (both runs show sel six-inch-1st.webp = the default/first depiction) - the page's aw.depSel read uses a different shape or overwrites at load; read web/index.html's depSel init and seed accordingly (matters only for the hide-on-place/restore-on-cancel checks). (c) the "no reqfail outside /tiles/" check tripped on Esri URLs because Esri paths are 'MapServer/tile/' (singular) and the filter only excluded '/tiles/' - allow 'arcgisonline' in the filter (the committed tests tolerate Esri aborts the same way).

**The three iter-127 residual failures, resolved (all in /tmp/fkverify/):**

1. **One-site line:** build_tree.py now Pops the server with the checkout's VENV python (`/home/agent/projects/ancient-works.a/.venv/bin/python` - our checkout has a .venv now; numpy/scipy/PIL verified). The line runs (~23 s), the overlay lands, the page's 90 s poll sees it, endFit closes the pane.
2. **The depSel seed - the real cause was MY PROBE, two bugs (found with a minimal diag, diag7.mjs):** (a) the addInitScript closure referenced Node-scope `site` - in the page context that is a ReferenceError swallowed by the try/catch (the committed part-3 pattern passes the site as an ARGUMENT and works - test_card_done_mark_moves_on proves addInitScript seeding works in this environment); (b) my fix then passed the object arg `{site, depFile}` but destructured `({sid, f})` - a KEY MISMATCH: the page-side object has keys site/depFile, the destructure wants sid/f -> both undefined -> the seed silently wrote `'{}'` to aw.depSel (the diag printed `OBJECT-ARG-undefined/undefined`). Fixed: pass `{sid: site, f: depFile}`. This playwright-core takes AT MOST ONE argument to addInitScript (same as evaluate): two args throws "Too many arguments"; and the object's keys must match the destructured names exactly or it fails silently inside the try/catch.
3. **reqfail filter:** added the 'arcgisonline' exclusion (Esri's URLs are .../MapServer/tile/... SINGULAR - the '/tiles/' exclusion misses them; in a no-internet headless run the doomed Esri fetches end as ERR_ABORTED, page-caused and expected - the same class the tests' own comments allow).

**The dep file to seed is six-inch-1st.webp, not os1inch.webp:** os1inch is kind 'map' year 1900 and loadDeps' surveyOldEnough (<= 2026-150) filters it out - the only depiction the page can show for this site is six-inch-1st (1842). (Run 2's "sel = the new plan overlay" was explained: after run 1's save the overlay is kind 'plan' in DRAWING and becomes the default pick; with the fixed seed six-inch-1st is restored exactly on Cancel, incl. the _ink URL, which the check now computes the way applyDep does - `src.endsWith(file)` is false for the ink variant.)

**The overlay m_per_px check was wrong units:** pipeline/plan_fit.py's fit.m_per_px is in WEBP-px units (pairs scaled by fs = image/doc.size, solved, divided by the downscale f = min(1, MAX_PX / max crop px), MAX_PX = 2400 at plan_fit.py:44) - measured 0.8849 vs the anchor's 0.4205 (ratio ~2.1 = the downscale 2400/5050). Replaced with two checks: (a) DOC level - in-page simFit over doc.pairs (4-tuples converted to {u,v,lon,lat} objects - simFit reads pair objects; raw arrays give all-nulls) must reproduce the anchor within the doc's rounding (mpx 1e-3 abs, rot 0.01 deg, maxResid < 2) - this is the box's "scale and rotation agreeing with the anchor within rounding"; (b) the overlay's m_per_px agrees with anchor.mpx * fs / f within 1e-3 rel (proves the overlay is the same similarity).

## 2026-10-12 307bb DECISION: iteration 138 - verification session (the lead's cut still stands; NO code written, working tree clean, nothing run). Two facts added to the hand-over design; the next session is the mechanical one (write the part-4 section per the hand-over, run the file, commit, tick boxes)
- **The m_per_px formula was DERIVED from pipeline/plan_fit.py this session, not copied from the hand-over**: `load_picture` (L203-222) returns `fs = im.width / doc.size[0]` (the raw file over the doc's size frame); build() (L262-336) scales pairs and crop by fs into pic-px, solves `m_px = |a|*k` in pic-px, then `f = min(1, MAX_PX/max(crop.size))` with `m_px /= f` when f<1 (webp px are f pic-px), stored `m_per_px` rounded to 4dp, `rot_deg` rounded to 2dp, `resid_m` rounded to 0.1 m ground. Hence `fit.m_per_px == anchor.mpx * fs / f` with f in pic-px crop units - the hand-over's `want_mpx = anchor.mpx * fs / f_scale` is exactly this; the round-to-4dp adds ~2.6e-4 relative, inside the 1e-3 tolerance (for the pick fs = 1 exactly, f ~= 2400/3081 ~= 0.779).

## 2026-10-12 309 DECISION: first session - investigation only; the check will be a stdlib module (tools/right_place.py, to be added to Touches) because the owner's Mac has no PIL, and the decisive offline signal for the Brighton case is the book's own list-of-figures section (county-organized), not a gazetteer
Task 309 (carved from 307) investigated. Facts established by running the real data (.venv/bin/python): (1) the matcher's namesake trap for Allcroft fig 217 is an exact-name tie ("Devil's Dyke Camp" -> "devil dyke" == Q3307343's name, score 1.0, runner_up 0.4) with an EMPTY place dict - Brighton is a city, and place_of() only extracts county/state words, so no contradiction check can fire inside books_match; the evidence contract is satisfied by the namesake, so 309's check is new logic. (2) The right site for the picture EXISTS in the catalog: devil-s-dyke-hillfort-q17674160 (Q17674160, West Sussex/Poynings). (3) data/books/lists/earthworkenglan01allcgoog.json's figures list is organized by county: fig 212 states Sussex, figs 213-223 (incl. 217) are county-free, fig 224 states Somerset - the section county of fig 217 is sussex; this is the picture's own metadata and the offline resolution that flags "near Brighton" vs Cambridgeshire. (4) Recorded evidence conflicts in review candidates are NOISY: true namesakes (bethlehem NL vs Holy Land x17, alauna->Ardoch 174km, cenabum->New Orleans, york-castle->Broome County NY) sit next to garbage resolutions (stonehenge "Larned" 7252km - an author name; fletio "Wit" the cartographer; glevum "Bristol" resolving to a US Bristol 5282km; durocortorum "Reims" 59km just over the 50km tolerance) - H3 (conflict>50km) needs a plausibility filter on named_place, decided by eyeballing the full flag list. (5) sites.json ids differ from catalog Q-ids for the SAME place (marietta, forthill, newark vs their Q-id catalog rows, ~150m apart) - any tied-away check needs the 50km distance guard or 12 candidates false-flag. (6) Sites with no catalog admin county (leeds-castle, fairlawne, chevening, chiswick-house) must never be flagged on a county word (unknown site county = no contradiction); my first throwaway script had exactly this bug. (7) tools/fit_queue.py must stay stdlib-only (owner's Mac, no PIL; even this VM's system python3 lacks PIL) so pipeline/books_match CANNOT be imported - a stdlib copy of its core goes in a new tools/right_place.py (added to Touches), guarded by a venv-only drift test against the real matcher. (8) test_fit_queue_synthetic's tree has only the four inputs - the check must be a no-op when the extra stores are absent (keep that test passing unmodified); test_fit_queue_real derives the entry set from the four inputs alone and WILL need updating with the same check. Design draft and the exact next steps (incl. the section-walk re-verification: the throwaway walk mis-keyed entries and returned somerset for fig 217) are in the task hand-over.

## 2026-10-12 309 DECISION: iteration 95 - tools/right_place.py written and run: 324 queue pairings checked, 15 flagged, all verified true namesake-place errors; six forced deviations from the session-3 spec
Session 4. Merged main first (conflict only in this task file; merge 6a5a8f8). Tests at merge: 3 failures, re-run in the main checkout and failing there too -> all pre-existing on main (the lead's matcher-fit wiring b22a2d2/9324f8b): test_fit_provenance::test_line_refuses_unmarked_non_auto_doc, test_fit_queue::test_save_runs_the_one_site_line, test_site_done::test_site_done_line; the driver-noted test_gate failure passed in my run. The verbatim core was re-extracted with the surviving /tmp/extract_core.py (18 blocks byte-verified); the module was assembled from /tmp/rp_top.py + /tmp/matcher_core.txt + /tmp/rp_bottom.py into tools/right_place.py (UNCOMMITTED - the harness saved it; commit it next session, then re-run the suite; expect exactly those same 3 main-side failures and nothing new). The full 15-flag list, the must-not-flag verification and the stale-expectation notes are in the task hand-over. The deviations from the session-3 spec (all forced by running it - where the spec text contradicted its own verified probes, the probes won):
1. word_counties filter: the spec said "not in TOWN_STOP/PLACE_WORDS/UK_COUNTIES_EXT", but PLACE_WORDS contains 'york' (the word-split of the US state "new york"), which would kill the york probe and the n0438_1 flag. The index filter is now len>=4, not digit, not in TOWN_STOP, not in UK_COUNTIES_EXT; the <=4-counties filter stays as the over-broad net ('king'/'little' drop there). Consequence: 'york' -> {yorkshire} ONLY - the catalog has no New York state sites, so the spec's {yorkshire, new york} expectation is stale, not a bug.
2. Town-word filter: TOWN_STOP | UK_COUNTIES_EXT | UK_ABBR (not PLACE_WORDS, same 'york' reason); US state words may be towns (they resolve to their own state through the catalog; explicit ones are caught by H1 via place_of anyway). 'yorks' (an abbreviation) is excluded from town words and counted only as the explicit word.
3. H2 applies only to book rows (the plan id is in data/books/index.jsonl). The first full run flagged 24: the 15 genuine book-row namesakes plus 9 commons false positives (dunster x1, oakham x2, pontefract x5, stonehenge x1 - descriptive file titles like "The new Larned History for ready reference, reading and research..." whose prose words happen to hit a county). Commons captions are descriptions, not "Name, Place" titles; H1 still applies to commons and is safe there (South Staffordshire Coalfield vs Dudley passes because west midlands->staffordshire).
4. find_right_site min_score=0.5 (new parameter, default): without it the "right" list displayed 0.06-0.39 junk (Woolwich Power Station for ARDOCH, Caus Castle for Pontefract). The matcher accepts at 0.75; 0.5 is already lenient for a move target. Brighton's hillfort (0.65) stays.
5. find_right_site place gate: the spec always used place={"uk_county": single implied}, but for a US picture that makes score_candidate reject every US site (its `if place.get("uk_county"): return None` branch). Fixed: implied is split into UK_CLASSICS/US_CLASSICS; a single UK implied becomes place.uk_county, a single US implied becomes place.state; several implied -> {} plus the gate (site classic county/state must be None or in implied).
6. Data-shape bugs found by the run: norm() does not compress whitespace, so "Bournemouth, Christchurch and Poole" becomes a double-space value - _classic now compresses after norm and the CLASSIC_UNITS keys are space-form ("southend on sea", "stockton on tees"). The spec's table was missing two values actually in the catalog: "conwy county borough"->caernarvonshire and "isle of anglesey"->anglesey (added; the unmapped list is now EMPTY on the current catalog - the CLI prints no warning).

Wiring shape fixed in 804's spec: build() is two-phase - every flag decision computed over the PRE-MUTATION candidate sets, then drops/moves/upserts applied, then entries recomputed - so a move into a site cannot feed that site's own flag decision in the same build. Disposition: built right site T -> the row (sites narrowed to [T]) joins T's entry in memory; unbuilt T -> upsert into T's item; no right site -> upsert into the source item (fit/review.upsert_item; imported the way tools/site_done.py imports review, sys.path insert of ROOT/fit). Upserts idempotent-by-value (reason deterministic from stored fields); a flagged candidate already in the source's own item stays out of the queue and is not re-upserted (steady state). A no-picture item that gains a moved first candidate gets renamed to <site>.<plan>.json (upsert_item's lead rule) - called out in the spec.

The driver re-handed 309 (split) while both subtasks sat in Tasks-in-flight (open) but NOT in Next-open-tasks, while the other split's subtasks 207a/207b (both `Depends on: 200`, done) WERE in Next-open-tasks. Diagnosis: I had given 804 `Depends on: 309` and 805 `Depends on: 309, 804` when splitting (session 6, commit 54a9d44). A split parent only becomes done AFTER its subtasks finish (the driver returns it then for the tick work), so a dependency on it can never be satisfied - a deadlock, not a wait (contrast 206, whose deps 211/218 were externally resolvable, so 206's 20 bring-backs were just waiting). Every earlier split in the repo uses the opposite convention - subtasks never depend on the split parent: 203a (001, 200, 201, 202, 802, 803) / 203b (203a); 207a/207b/207c (200); 213a (none) / 213b (213a); 302a (none) / 302b (302a) / 302c (302b) / 302d (302c); 307a (none, a record) / 307b (307a) / 307c (307b) / 307d (307c). The module-availability concern behind my 804->309 edge is handled without it: subtasks run in the parent's owner lane (agent c), whose branch already has fc67bb9 (the module) - the lane assumption is load-bearing, not redundant (verified this session: main head cf29be2, `git cat-file -e main:tools/right_place.py` = absent, so a branch that gets main merged does NOT have the module until 309 merges); both subtasks' Notes now carry the AGENTS.md runtime rule for the case a different agent's branch receives them (add `Depends on: 309` at that point, commit, wait - never copy the module). 805's `Depends on: 309` was pure duplication of its 804 edge (after 804 merges, the chain carries both the wiring and the module) - cut to `Depends on: 804`, which is its real ordering need (the rebuild runs after the wiring merges). Also removed the now-satisfied `Depends on: 305` from 309 itself (305 is done; the 302a precedent: remove satisfied deps from your own line). Authority: AGENTS.md - "Rewrite, reorder (rename the letters), add or drop your subtasks whenever you learn more" - both files are mine and unmodified by anyone since 54a9d44; the lead has precedent for cutting dead edges from the task graph (0be0b09 on 206: "a cycle that kept 206 open") but that is a human-side action I did not need to ask for, because these are my own subtasks' lines and the fix follows the repo's dominant convention. Baseline re-verified in the same session: `python3 tools/right_place.py --all` -> 324 pairings, 15 flagged (identical to iterations 95/109/97), my data area byte-identical since fc67bb9; no test reads tasks/ (grep confirmed), so this change cannot affect the suite. Expected driver behavior after this commit: 804 (Depends on: none) enters Next-open-tasks and is handed out in order - to agent c, whose branch has the module; 805 follows when 804 merges; when both are done the driver returns 309 and I tick its three carved boxes and set it done.

data/commons/files.jsonl; dump kept in the task hand-over). They fall into: ~85-90 rows that

the existing one-search-per-name + batched-claims prefetch to within 50 km); both store the

## 2026-10-11 801 DECISION: Clifford's Tower has P625 (6 m) - the part route adds 40 passes; part-without-P625 falls back to the site's own coords (logged, not a loosening)
Iter 15 (agent c). Measured P625 of all 343 unique catalog has_part qids (2 full batched runs + 5 spot

part, so this is a safety net, not a coverage driver; the 50 km check still applies wherever the part
carries real P625.

## 2026-10-11 801 DECISION: offline candidate prototype done - extractor rules finalized (NFKD mandatory; pairs-before-singles order; title-only-pass rejected)
Iter 16 (agent c). Built /tmp/sheet_proto.py (v1) and /tmp/sheet_proto2.py (v2, throwaway, no repo
changes) and eyeballed ALL 354 stored-none rows per site against the iter-14 census. Findings that
changed the iter-15 design:
1. NFKD-normalize BEFORE tokenizing - mandatory. v1 tokenized raw text: "Orléans" -> "O"+"rl"+"ans"
   (zero candidates), which would have cost the 4 cenabum pass rows; "Bethléem"/"Maastricht" variants
   likewise. Candidates are stored in the normalized form (the check's norm-substring test stays
   valid because check() normalizes both sides).
2. Strip the "File:" prefix and truncate desc at a word boundary at 600 chars (a cut word like
   "Bethl" becomes a phantom candidate otherwise). Digits are never kept tokens (v1 bug: "000",
   "1870" leaked and formed junk pairs).
3. Run splitting: a comma OR a real lowercase word (len>=3) between two kept words breaks the run.
   Keeps "New Orleans" / "Cranbourne Hampshire" / "Monmouth Castle" as phrases while breaking OS
   place lists (v1's greedy 3-merge turned them into unresolvable garbage and lost the singles).
4. Candidate order per run: 2-word pairs, then 3-word phrases, then singles. Decisive: the lone
   "Orleans" resolves to Orléans (the cenabum site itself); trying it before "New Orleans" would
   FALSE-PASS the New-Orleans junk rows. Pairs-first prevents that.
5. Verdict = the existing caption-route best/conflict logic over the candidate set (nearest <=50 km
   -> sheet_mention pass; nearest far -> none+conflict; else plain none). Title-preference for
   PASSES was tested and rejected: the cenabum New-Orleans junk names the place only in the DESC and
   box 2 demands the conflict there.
6. STOP list mined from the 354 rows' capitalized-word frequency table (EN metadata labels + NL/FR
   field words + adjectives English/Roman/...); NEVER stop place-name components (New, Great, Old,
   North/South, City, Church, Tower, River, Park, Garden). v2's list accidentally DROPPED the
   preposition block (The/Of/De/Van/La/Le...) and "ordnance/quartier/house/design/pattern/textile/
   numerisation/onderdeel" - those additions + the digit exclusion are still PENDING (my edit call
   failed atomically); they are listed in the task hand-over.
Expected after the fixes: ~1400-1600 unique candidates (v2 measured 2078); the tail is person names
(authors/artists/publishers of the ~180 book-scan rows) - harmless (no P625, the distance guard is
the mechanism), just one-off search cost (~25-35 min at 1 req/s).
Accepted consequence (logged per the iter-14 rule): a city name shared with a person/family name
("York" on the 1914 Christmas tin on york-castle, "Dudley" on a genealogy book) will pass when the
site sits in that city - keyword+50km cannot distinguish a depiction from a name mention; the
never-pass-far guarantee holds and box 1 only needs the majority of the genuinely-naming rows (the
40 Clifford rows pass via the part route, which runs before sheet).
Part-route check: EXACTLY 40 of the 354 none rows name a has_part of their own site (all
Clifford's Tower) - matches the iter-15 census.

## 2026-10-11 801 DECISION: check_sheet re-runs the extractor instead of a norm-substring check - agent c's "candidates are norm-substrings by construction" claim is false
Measured: 392 of the 1483 sheet candidates fail `norm(candidate) in norm(title+desc)` (e.g. "Robert Dennis" out of "Robert N. Dennis" - a 1-letter non-kept token survives in norm(hay) and splits the pair; "York Castle" out of "New York. Castle Garden"). check_sheet therefore requires the stored named_place to be in `sheet_candidates(title, desc)` (the extractor is deterministic and offline - the same re-derive-from-printed-text pattern check_book uses for the matcher). check_part keeps norm-substring (a part name is the catalog's own string, norm applied symmetrically both sides - always re-verifies). Pitfall filed separately.

## 2026-10-11 801 DECISION: an edit dropped the `def check_none(ev):` line (NameError at check runtime) - caught by the real-file check run before committing, restored in the same session
When inserting check_part/check_sheet after check_caption, the edit's oldText ended at `def check_none(ev):` but the newText did not repeat it, leaving check_none's body as orphaned dead code under check_sheet and a NameError in check_commons. Symptom: `fit/evidence.py check` dies with `NameError: name 'check_none' is not defined`. Lesson: when a block insertion lands between two functions, the trailing `def` line of the NEXT function belongs in the newText.

## 2026-10-11 801 DECISION: sheet verdict is a FIRST-RESOLVED WALK, not "nearest <=50 km wins" (iter-16's two statements were contradictory)
Iter 17 (agent c). Iter-16 said both "nearest <=50 km -> pass; nearest far -> conflict" and "pairs-before-
singles prevents the New-Orleans false pass". Both true only if the walk is order-dependent: iterate the
ordered candidates (per run: pairs, then 3-word phrases, then singles; title before desc) and let the
FIRST candidate that resolves decide the row (<=50 km pass / >50 km conflict); unresolved candidates are
skipped. Verified the "nearest wins" variant is WRONG on three real rows: "York" (a word inside "New
York") resolves ~2 km from york-castle and would pass the 4 NYC "Castle Garden" rows; "Orleans" (inside
"New Orleans") resolves onto cenabum itself and would pass the 13 New-Orleans rows; "York Castle" (glued
from "New York. Castle Garden") resolves to the site itself and would pass the same 4 rows. Routes cascade
caption -> part -> sheet; the first route returning pass or conflict decides (a caption conflict preempts
sheet, e.g. the 2 "detail Limmel" rows whose caption names "Bethlehem" -> Q5776 ~4700 km; a 3rd Limmel row
without that word still conflicts, because Maastricht is ~290 km from bethlehem-q2523914's stored point -
6.61E 53.38N is NOT near Maastricht).

## 2026-10-11 801 DECISION: castrum-levefanum is the DUTCH Limes fort, not Beauvais - the "Peutinger Casaromago" rows conflict on BOTH limes sites (census correction)
Iter 17 (agent c). The iter-14 census expected the "Peutinger Casaromago.jpg" row to PASS on
castrum-levefanum "~Beauvais". Wrong: the catalog site (Q1309559) is stored at (5.353E, 51.963N) - the
Dutch Lower German Limes identification (region netherlands, part_of Q1265889), 314-368 km from
Beauvais/Amiens. Live-checked this session (3 small wbsearchentities/wbgetentities queries): "Casaromago"
and "Sammarobriva" have NO Wikidata items (search returns []), and "Peutinger" first-P625 is Q2081791
(Peutinger-Gymnasium Ellwangen, 10.13E 48.97N) = 475-500 km from both Dutch sites. So the first-resolved
walk lands on "Peutinger" (far) -> the row records kind none WITH a conflict on BOTH fletio and
castrum-levefanum (iter-14's "passes on castrum-levefanum ~Beauvais" is void; fletio's "conflict-none"
holding). Net effect: ~1-2 fewer genuinely-naming rows pass than the iter-14 census said (~83-88 of
~85-90 instead of the full set) - the box-1 majority still holds. Lesson: never assume a site's geography
from its name; the catalog stores the Dutch identification. (PITFALLS entries for this fact and the
Latin-name/no-item fact are PENDING - the context limit cut the session before PITFALLS.md could be
edited; add them next session.)

      (Depends on: 001) is exactly what I implemented.

      `if not json.load(open(p)).get("auto")` - the same criterion as `fit/batch_us.py
      write_golden`; a doc without the field is a hand fit. The 20-doc precondition, the
      seed-20260108 derivation, the REGISTERED tables, `check_registered` and the five section-4

      partition) now say the name list is the non-automatic one. `tests/test_gate.py`: only the
      offline note in the docstring changed ("the 20 documents in data/db/platefits/" ->
      "data/db/platefits/"); no test logic or assertion touched. docs/gate.md and
      tests/golden/gate_heldout.txt are untouched (not in the diff).
WHY THE DOC STAYED: the task's Notes offered an optional section-1 clarification sentence. I chose
      not to add one: section 1 is the frozen pre-registration ("not to be changed") and never
      promised to count every .json in the directory; section 3's "the 20 hand-fit documents in
      data/db/platefits/" stays literally true (there are still 20; the 16 auto are a different
      kind of document). The clarification now lives in the tool's docstrings. If the driver or
      the human disagrees, adding one sentence to section 1 is the follow-up - recorded here so
      the decision is not re-litigated.
EVIDENCE (this checkout: 36 docs = 20 hand + 16 auto from 203a): non-auto names == REGISTERED

# Task 804 fit-queue-wire

## 2026-10-12 804 DECISION: iteration 100 (session 9) - the two-phase right-place wiring landed in tools/fit_queue.py (boxes 1+2) and verified on real data: 15 flags == CLI, queue 116->114, 5 new review items / 6 upserts carrying place_reason, steady-state zero churn; the code is UNCOMMITTED at hand-over (harness-saved) - the next session commits it first
Session 9 on the 309 lane. Worked 804 boxes 1+2 (the wiring itself) in the working tree on top of the session-8 refactor (d413d0e); the context limit hit before the commit, so the tools/fit_queue.py diff is harness-saved and UNCOMMITTED - git status at hand-over shows exactly that one modified file (no data files, no agent-a files).
1. What landed: header docstring (the namesake rule; the stdlib-only line names right_place/review as the only imports); self sys.path inserts of ROOT/tools and ROOT/fit before `import right_place` / `import review` (noqa E402, the site_done pattern - required under the runpy GUARD, PITFALLS); _site_records (sites.json keyed by id, site_facts' input); _place_store (right_place.load, None when none of catalog/index/lists present -> build is exactly 305's, the no-op degrade); _place_flags (one site_facts per site, picture_facts+check_pairing per candidate, over the PRE-MUTATION maps -> {(sid, plan): (facts, reason)}); _item_candidate; _place_entry (the item's stored fields as a dict copy when item-origin - pairs/evidence/why travel with it - else the row's fields, batch_us._entry's shape with the books/<ia>/<file> url fallback; plus plan and the reason under the NEW key place_reason); _apply_flags (sorted flag order: drop the plan, a site emptied leaves the queue; top of find_right_site (min_score 0.5) = T: T queue-eligible -> dict-copy of the row with sites narrowed to [T] joins T's map, else upsert into T's item (or S's own item when no passing T), SKIPPED when that item already holds the plan; a per-build moved set stops duplicate (target, plan) writes); build() = maps -> store (None -> no-op) -> flags -> apply -> _entry; final sort and {at, entries} envelope untouched; the build never calls review.export and never removes a candidate from an item.
2. Design decisions (refinements of the box wording, all deliberate): (a) "T is built" for the join-entry branch = T is queue-ELIGIBLE (in sites.json + dir exists + no hand doc, the eligible set from built/hand), not merely "web/data/sites/<T>/ exists" as box 2's words say - a move into a hand-fit site (or a dir absent from sites.json) has no entry to join and would silently lose the candidate; the refinement is inert on today's data (every right site is unbuilt) and box 4's synthetic test covers the entry branch. (b) The reason's key is place_reason - a new extra (the batch's why/gate_score stay untouched; a candidate can carry both); the 804 box-3 README note documents it, and 307c (agent a) will display it from this key. (c) The never-rewrite-held-item skip (box 2's steady-state rule, specified only for S's own item) was extended to the T-item branch too: a batch's own entry in a T item carries pairs/evidence/coarse that the thinner move entry would erase, and a re-run's entry is byte-identical anyway, so skipping is exactly the no-churn steady state the box wants. (d) The build never removes a flagged candidate from the source item (the "both stay in review forever" decision stands) - the flag+drop is recomputed from stored fields every build, so the queue stays clean indefinitely.
3. Verification (all in temp trees; the checkout's data stayed untouched): (i) part-1 tests pass UNMODIFIED in 0.68 s (the synthetic tree has no catalog/books = pure no-op; the current four-inputs-only test_fit_queue_real is a no-op too); (ii) real data (four 305 inputs + data/catalog + data/books, build() run in-process twice): the build's flags == the CLI's set EXACTLY (15; `python3 tools/right_place.py --all` on the checkout still 324 checked / 15 flagged); queue 116 -> 114 entries (vindomora and windmill-hill leave entirely - every candidate flagged; devil-s-dyke keeps only n0662_1, the owner's Brighton case out of the Cambridgeshire dyke's entry; bokerley-dyke keeps n0174_1; must-not-flag set untouched); EXACTLY 5 new review-item files / 6 upserts each with place_reason and the row's url (saucer-barrow...n-q17677473 gets n0558_1 AND n0558_2, castle-dyke-q31112898 n0599_1, devil-s-dyke-hillfort-q17674160 n0686_1, down-end-castle-q5302812 n0285_1, oval-barrow...-q17672356 n0557_1 - 805's rebuild must reproduce exactly these); 341 existing item files unchanged; the second build's queue is identical (ex-at) and every item file byte-identical (zero churn); (iii) full suite `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ -q`: 3 failed / 77 passed / 1 skipped = baseline (the same three pre-existing main-side failures; no new failures).
4. Two small slips this session (both caught before any harm): an edit of the import comment dropped the second line's `#` (SyntaxError, caught by py_compile before any test ran), and a follow-up edit dropped the ROOT/tools sys.path insert line (caught by re-reading before the tests); the file compiles and the suite is green at hand-over. The 804/309 hand-over files were committed at the limit (7fd3ef5); the code diff is still to be committed next session.
